 The Reimer steps are justified by the following exploration observation;
the exact finite-product input is Lemma  2.2 . 
 
 
 Auxiliary Lemma 5.E   (Exploration disjointness) . 
 
 Let  D 0 D_{0}  be an allowed vertex set, let  S , T , F ⊆ D 0 S,T,F\subseteq D_{0} , and
let  u , w ∈ D 0 u,w\in D_{0} . For a finite set  Λ ⊆ D 0 \Lambda\subseteq D_{0}  containing
 u , w u,w , write  K u D 0 K_{u}^{D_{0}}  for the open cluster of  u u  in the
configuration induced on  D 0 D_{0} , and define 
 

 
 
 ℰ Λ \displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda} 
 = { u  is open , u ↔ w  in  S ∩ Λ , K u D 0 ⊆ Λ , K u D 0 ∩ F = ∅ } , \displaystyle=\{u\text{ is open},\ u\leftrightarrow w\text{ in }S\cap\Lambda,\ K_{u}^{D_{0}}\subseteq\Lambda,\ K_{u}^{D_{0}}\cap F=\varnothing\}, 
 
 
 
 ℬ Λ \displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda} 
 = { T ↔ F ​  by an open path in  ​ D 0 ∩ Λ } . \displaystyle=\{T\leftrightarrow F\text{ by an open path in }D_{0}\cap\Lambda\}. 
 
 
 Then these are finite-coordinate events and 
 

 
 
 ℰ Λ ∩ ℬ Λ ⊆ ℰ Λ □ ℬ Λ , \mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}\cap\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}\mathbin{\square}\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}, 
 
 
 where  □ \square  denotes disjoint occurrence in the site coordinates. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 On  ℰ Λ \mathcal{E}_{\Lambda} , expose the exact set
 C = K u D 0 C=K_{u}^{D_{0}} . The event  ℰ Λ \mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}  is certified by the
open sites of  C C , the closed sites of its relative external boundary
 ∂ V D 0 C \partial^{D_{0}}_{V}C , and an open  u u -to- w w  path in
 S ∩ C ∩ Λ S\cap C\cap\Lambda . Let  P ⊆ D 0 ∩ Λ P\subseteq D_{0}\cap\Lambda  be an open
 T T -to- F F  path certifying  ℬ Λ \mathcal{B}_{\Lambda} . The path  P P 
cannot meet  C C , since otherwise its terminal segment to  F F ,
concatenated with an open path in  C C  from  u u , would give
 K u D 0 ∩ F ≠ ∅ K_{u}^{D_{0}}\cap F\neq\varnothing . Nor can  P P  meet
 ∂ V D 0 C \partial^{D_{0}}_{V}C , whose sites are certified closed. Hence the two
finite coordinate certificates are disjoint, proving the asserted
inclusion. Lemma  2.2  may therefore be applied. 
 
 
 At the infinite-volume use below, the exact-cluster exhaustion in
( 5.78 ) supplies precisely these finite events; no continuity
of disjoint occurrence is invoked. The nonunimodular proof uses Reimer
only in the simplified and general peak-comparison identities
 [ 15 , Equations (6.10) and (6.20)] . In the
general identity the bounded connector is forced open, while the at most
 d ​ | γ | d|\gamma|  neighboring sites that could create the forbidden
connection are forced closed. This costs a positive
 ( G , p ) (G,p) -dependent constant.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Fourth, in the comparison between a  p c s − ε p_{c}^{\rm s}-\varepsilon  cluster
 A A  and the  p c s p_{c}^{\rm s}  configuration, use its external vertex
boundary. It has at most  d ​ | A | d|A|  sites. Closing the near-top part costs
at least 
 

 
 
 ( 1 − p c s 1 − p c s + ε ) d ​ | A | . \left(\frac{1-p_{c}^{\rm s}}{1-p_{c}^{\rm s}+\varepsilon}\right)^{d|A|}. 
 
 (5.27) 
 
 For the far part, assign each boundary site to its first incident
vertex of  A A ; the required nonconnection events are decreasing, so
site FKG gives the same product lower bound. The finite-energy estimate
in  [ 15 , Lemma 5.16]  needs a little more care.
Given the restricted cluster  K k K_{k}  and its top set  Z k Z_{k} , choose
 u ∈ Z k u\in Z_{k}  measurably from the explored data and use the neighbor  u + u^{+} 
from ( 5.7 ). The exploration is confined to  L − ∞ , k ​ ( v ) L_{-\infty,k}(v) , whereas
 u + ∈ L k + 1 ​ ( v ) u^{+}\in L_{k+1}(v) ; hence its label is untested and remains Bernoulli
 p p  conditionally on the exploration. Open  u + u^{+} , and close every
neighbor of  Z k ∪ { u + } Z_{k}\cup\{u^{+}\}  outside  K k ∪ { u + } K_{k}\cup\{u^{+}\}  that has not
already been tested closed. No site in this closing set was tested open:
such a site would have entered  K k K_{k}  through a retained exploration edge;
the only omitted edges have both endpoints in  L k ​ ( v ) L_{k}(v) , and their other
endpoints are untested unless reached elsewhere. There are at most
 d ⁡ ( | Z k | + 1 ) d(|Z_{k}|+1)  newly prescribed closed sites. This is a single
exploration in a layer-determined graph, with no earlier query history,
so it is the case  Q old = ∅ Q_{\rm old}=\varnothing  of the corrected kernel
rather than an adaptive reset of previously queried labels. The cluster
kernel ( 5.36 ) therefore gives, conditionally on the complete
exploration data, 
 

 
 
 p ​ ( 1 − p ) d ⁡ ( | Z k | + 1 ) . p(1-p)^{d(|Z_{k}|+1)}. 
 
 (5.28) 
 
 Formula ( 5.28 ), combined with the conditional bound on  | Z k | |Z_{k}| , is
used explicitly in ( 5.63 )–( 5.72 ) below; no peak estimate is inferred
at this stage. 
 
 
 Finally, we need the following site version of the critical-cluster
fact used in the height analysis. 
 
 
 Auxiliary Lemma 5.F   (Critical clusters) . 
 
 If  G G  satisfies the standing
hypotheses of this section, then Bernoulli site percolation on  G G  at
 p c s p_{c}^{\rm s}  has no infinite cluster. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The bond-coordinate analogue follows from
 [ 13 , Theorem 1] . For sites, we
use two arguments whose coordinate status can be checked exactly. First,
Timár’s critical theorem
 [ 23 , Corollary 5.7 and Remark 5.11] , formulated using the full
automorphism group, says that a nonunimodular transitive graph cannot have
infinitely many infinite clusters at critical Bernoulli percolation. Its
hypothesis is exactly the standing canonical-action hypothesis above.
Although stated in edge notation,  [ 23 , Remark 5.11]  explicitly
says that all arguments in the relevant sections repeat for site
percolation: they require only insertion and deletion tolerance. The
Newman–Schulman zero–one–infinity trichotomy, in the site form
recorded for transitive graphs in  [ 17 , Theorem 7.5] ,
therefore reduces the claim to excluding a unique infinite site cluster.
For this case, the canonical nonunimodular action implies that  G G  is
nonamenable  [ 3 , Theorem 3.4] . Since  G G 
has bounded degree, it therefore has exponential volume growth, so
 g := lim inf r | B r | 1 / r > 1 g:=\liminf_{r}|B_{r}|^{1/r}>1 . Let 
 

 
 
 κ ^ p ​ ( r ) = p − 1 ​ inf d ⁡ ( x , y ) ≤ r ℙ p ​ ( x ↔ y ) . \widehat{\kappa}_{p}(r)=p^{-1}\inf_{d(x,y)\leq r}\mathbb{P}_{p}(x\leftrightarrow y). 
 
 
 If  d ⁡ ( x , y ) ≤ r + s d(x,y)\leq r+s , choose  z z  with  d ⁡ ( x , z ) ≤ r d(x,z)\leq r  and
 d ⁡ ( z , y ) ≤ s d(z,y)\leq s . Conditional on  z z  being open, site FKG gives
 κ ^ p ​ ( r + s ) ≥ κ ^ p ​ ( r ) ​ κ ^ p ​ ( s ) \widehat{\kappa}_{p}(r+s)\geq\widehat{\kappa}_{p}(r)\widehat{\kappa}_{p}(s) . For  p < p c s p<p_{c}^{\rm s} ,
site sharpness  [ 2 , Theorem 2 and Section 6] 
and exponential growth give 
 

 
 
 p ​ | B r | ​ κ ^ p ​ ( r ) ≤ χ p < ∞ . p|B_{r}|\widehat{\kappa}_{p}(r)\leq\chi_{p}<\infty. 
 
 
 If  g = lim inf r | B r | 1 / r > 1 g=\liminf_{r}|B_{r}|^{1/r}>1 , supermultiplicativity and Fekete’s
lemma imply  κ ^ p ​ ( r ) ≤ g − r \widehat{\kappa}_{p}(r)\leq g^{-r}  for every fixed
 p < p c s p<p_{c}^{\rm s}  and  r ≥ 1 r\geq 1 . Couple by uniform labels and let
 p ↑ p c s p\uparrow p_{c}^{\rm s} . For each fixed pair  x , y x,y , the events
 { x ↔ y } \{x\leftrightarrow y\}  increase to their event at criticality; there
are only finitely many pair orbits at distance at most  r r . Hence 
 

 
 
 sup r ≥ 1 κ ^ p c s ​ ( r ) 1 / r ≤ g − 1 < 1 . \sup_{r\geq 1}\widehat{\kappa}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}(r)^{1/r}\leq g^{-1}<1. 
 
 (5.29) 
 
 This is the normalized site version of the connectivity-decay proof for
graphs of exponential growth. 
 
 
 If the critical site configuration had a unique infinite cluster, site
FKG would give 
 

 
 
 ℙ p c s ​ ( x ↔ y ) ≥ ℙ ⁡ ( x ↔ ∞ ) ​ ℙ ​ ( y ↔ ∞ ) , \mathbb{P}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}(x\leftrightarrow y)\geq\mathbb{P}(x\leftrightarrow\infty)\mathbb{P}(y\leftrightarrow\infty), 
 
 
 whose right side is uniformly positive by transitivity. This contradicts
( 5.29 ). The site trichotomy
 [ 17 , Theorem 7.5]  and Timár’s site-valid critical
theorem  [ 23 , Corollary 5.7 and Remark 5.11]  now leave zero
infinite clusters.
∎ 
 
 
 
 We will also use that  p c s < 1 p_{c}^{\rm s}<1 . By the same nonamenability,
let  h E > 0 h_{E}>0  be the edge-isoperimetric constant of  G G . Every finite connected
 A ∋ o A\ni o  then has
 | ∂ V A | ≥ h E ​ | A | / d |\partial_{V}A|\geq h_{E}|A|/d , while the number of connected
 n n -vertex sets containing  o o  is at most  ( e ​ d ) n − 1 (ed)^{n-1} . Conditional
on  o o  being open, the event  K o = A K_{o}=A  forces every site of
 ∂ V A \partial_{V}A  closed. Consequently 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p o ​ ( | K o | < ∞ ) ≤ ∑ n ≥ 1 ( e ​ d ) n − 1 ​ ( 1 − p ) h E ​ n / d < 1 \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{o}(|K_{o}|<\infty)\leq\sum_{n\geq 1}(ed)^{n-1}(1-p)^{h_{E}n/d}<1 
 
 
 when  p < 1 p<1  is sufficiently close to one. Hence
 p c s < 1 p_{c}^{\rm s}<1 , and every finite-energy constant used below at
criticality is strictly positive. 
 
 
 Under the conditional-root law, the root-peak event also has a uniform
positive denominator: it contains the event that all  d d  neighbors of
the conditioned-open root are closed, of probability  ( 1 − p ) d (1-p)^{d} .
This is the site replacement for the isolated-root event in the
fractional-moment bootstrap. 
 
 
 We also record explicitly why conditioning the root open is compatible
with every tilted mass transport. If  F ⁡ ( x , y , ω ) F(x,y,\omega)  is diagonally
invariant, integrable, and vanishes unless both  x x  and  y y  are open,
then the ordinary tilted mass-transport principle and transitivity give 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v ​ ∑ x F ⁡ ( v , x , ω ) = 𝔼 ^ p v ​ ∑ x F ⁡ ( x , v , ω ) ​ Δ ​ ( v , x ) . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v}\sum_{x}F(v,x,\omega)=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v}\sum_{x}F(x,v,\omega)\Delta(v,x). 
 
 (5.30) 
 
 Indeed, multiply both sides by  p p . Since the summand on each side
forces  v v  open, the resulting identity is exactly the unconditioned
tilted mass-transport identity. The same proof applies to signed
transports with integrable absolute value. Every transport below has
this endpoint support because it transports mass only between vertices
in the same open cluster. Thus no change of root under mass transport
introduces an unaccounted factor of  p p . 
 
 
 The transports used below are: the
susceptibility symmetry (mass between two connected vertices); the
large- Δ \Delta  half of the ghost estimate (the indicator
 x ↔ y x\leftrightarrow y ); the upward/downward identity defining
 β p \beta_{p} ; the transport from a cluster peak to a lower-layer cluster
vertex; and the Holder transport ( 5.92 ). Each displayed summand contains
the connection of its two arguments. Root-orbit averaging and the random
layer offset are independent of site states, so neither adds another
case to ( 5.30 ). 
 
 
 We can now state the nonunimodular estimates used below.
Put 
 

 
 
 L m , n ( v ) = ⋃ m ≤ j ≤ n L j ( v ) , X j m , n ( v ) = | { x ∈ L j ( v ) : v ↔ x  in  L m , n ( v ) } | , L_{m,n}(v)=\bigcup_{m\leq j\leq n}L_{j}(v),\qquad X_{j}^{m,n}(v)=\left|\left\{x\in L_{j}(v):v\leftrightarrow x\text{ in }L_{m,n}(v)\right\}\right|, 
 
 
 where infinite endpoints are allowed. For  0 ≤ a ≤ 1 0\leq a\leq 1 , define 
 

 
 
 E ^ p m , n ​ ( j , a ) = sup v ∈ V , x ∈ [ 0 , 1 ) 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( X j m , n ​ ( v ) ) a ] , \widehat{E}_{p}^{m,n}(j;a)=\sup_{v\in V,\,x\in[0,1)}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}\left[(X_{j}^{m,n}(v))^{a}\right], 
 
 (5.31) 
 
 with  0 0 = 0 0^{0}=0 , and let
 E ^ p m , n ​ ( j ) = E ^ p m , n ​ ( j , 1 ) \widehat{E}_{p}^{m,n}(j)=\widehat{E}_{p}^{m,n}(j;1) . Let 
 

 
 
 I h s = { p > 0 : E ^ p − ∞ , n ​ ( j , 1 ) < ∞ ​  for every  − ∞ < j ≤ n < ∞ } . I_{h}^{\rm s}=\left\{p>0:\widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,n}(j;1)<\infty\text{ for every }-\infty<j\leq n<\infty\right\}. 
 
 
 For  p ∈ I h s p\in I_{h}^{\rm s} , let  β p s \beta_{p}^{\rm s}  be the common value 
 

 
 
 β p s = − lim k → ∞ 1 t 0 ​ k log E ^ p − ∞ , k ( k ; 1 ) = 1 − lim k → ∞ 1 t 0 ​ k ​ log ⁡ E ^ p − ∞ , 0 ​ ( − k , 1 ) . \begin{split}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}&=-\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{t_{0}k}\log\widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,k}(k;1)\\
&=1-\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{t_{0}k}\log\widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,0}(-k;1).\end{split} 
 
 (5.32) 
 
 
 
 We use no pointwise interpretation of an unspecified  o ⁡ ( k ) o(k)  below.
For a family  F v , x ​ ( k ) F_{v,x}(k) , the assertion 
 

 
 
 sup v , x F v , x ​ ( k ) ≤ C a ​ exp ⁡ [ c ​ k + o a ​ ( k ) ] \sup_{v,x}F_{v,x}(k)\leq C_{a}\exp[ck+o_{a}(k)] 
 
 
 means the following quantified statement: for every  ζ > 0 \zeta>0  there
is  C a , ζ < ∞ C_{a,\zeta}<\infty , independent of  v , x , k v,x,k , such that 
 

 
 
 sup v , x F v , x ​ ( k ) ≤ C a , ζ ​ e ( c + ζ ) ​ k ( k ≥ 0 ) . \sup_{v,x}F_{v,x}(k)\leq C_{a,\zeta}e^{(c+\zeta)k}\quad(k\geq 0). 
 
 (5.33) 
 
 The same convention applies with additional slab widths  r r : their
contribution is  e C 0 ​ r e^{C_{0}r} , where  C 0 = C 0 ​ ( G , p ) C_{0}=C_{0}(G,p)  is independent of
the fractional exponent. This is generic notation only. When a later
argument distinguishes the shift loss from the excess-width loss, we use
the named constants  C sh C_{\rm sh}  and  C ⋆ C_{\star} , respectively, rather
than identifying either one implicitly with  C 0 C_{0} . Finite sums,
products, fixed positive powers,
translations by a bounded number of layers, and exponentially convergent
convolutions preserve ( 5.33 ): divide the requested  ζ \zeta  by the
finite number of input factors and absorb bounded indices into the
constant. This elementary observation will be called the
 uniform error calculus . In the fractional iteration leading to a fixed final
exponent, only finitely many auxiliary exponents are used; hence the
maximum of their constants is finite and ( 5.33 ) remains uniform in
the root and offset. 
 
 
 Lemma 5.2   (Nonunimodular site estimates) . 
 
 Let  G G  be an
infinite, connected, locally finite, vertex-transitive graph whose full
automorphism action  Γ = Aut ⁡ ( G ) \Gamma=\operatorname{Aut}(G)  is nonunimodular.
Consider independent Bernoulli site percolation and the independent
equivariant layer field ( 5.6 ) defined from this canonical action. Then: 
 
 
 1.  The two limits in ( 5.32 ) exist and agree whenever
 p ∈ I h s p\in I_{h}^{\rm s} . All estimates below are uniform in the root
 v v  and in the fixed layer offset  R v = x R_{v}=x . 
 
 
 2.  At  p = p c s p=p_{c}^{\rm s} , for every  0 < ε ≤ 1 0<\varepsilon\leq 1  and
 ζ > 0 \zeta>0 , there is  C ε , ζ < ∞ C_{\varepsilon,\zeta}<\infty , independent
of the root and layer offset, such that 
 

 
 
 E ^ p c s − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( k , 1 − ε ) ≤ C ε , ζ ​ { exp ⁡ [ − ( t 0 − ζ ) ​ k ] , k ≥ 0 , exp ⁡ [ ζ ​ | k | ] , k < 0 , \widehat{E}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{-\infty,\infty}(k;1-\varepsilon)\leq C_{\varepsilon,\zeta}\begin{cases}\exp[-(t_{0}-\zeta)k],&k\geq 0,\\
\exp[\zeta|k|],&k<0,\end{cases} 
 
 (5.34) 
 
 
 
 3.  For the conditional tilted susceptibility ( 5.13 ), 
 

 
 
 p c s < p c s ​ ( λ ) ≤ p t s ( 0 < λ < 1 ) . p_{c}^{\rm s}<p_{c}^{\rm s}(\lambda)\leq p_{t}^{\rm s}\qquad(0<\lambda<1). 
 
 (5.35) 
 
 
 
 4.  One has  p c s ∈ I h s p_{c}^{\rm s}\in I_{h}^{\rm s} ,
 β p c s s ≤ 1 \beta_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}\leq 1 ,
 α p s = β p s \alpha_{p}^{\rm s}=\beta_{p}^{\rm s}  for
 0 < p < p t s 0<p<p_{t}^{\rm s} , and
 α p c s s ≥ 1 \alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}\geq 1 . Consequently
 α p c s s = 1 \alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}=1 . 
 
 
 
 Proof.  Realize every conditional probability below on one label
space:  ( U z ) z ∈ V (U_{z})_{z\in V}  are i.i.d. uniform on  [ 0 , 1 ] [0,1] ,  z z  is
 p p -open when  U z ≤ p U_{z}\leq p , and the layer field is independent of all
labels. Conditioning on  R v = x R_{v}=x  means the canonical law obtained by
fixing that independent offset equal to  x x , then defining every other
offset equivariantly by ( 5.6 ). Thus the supremum over  x x  does not
depend on arbitrary versions of a regular conditional law. The following
three kernels are the complete list of conditional laws used in the
proof. 
 
 
 Cluster kernel.  Let  W ⊆ V W\subseteq V  be deterministic after the
layer field has been fixed, let  v ∈ W v\in W , and explore the  p p -cluster
of  v v  in  W W  by testing boundary sites one at a time. If a finite
outcome has open set  A ∋ v A\ni v  and tested external boundary
 D = ∂ V W A D=\partial_{V}^{W}A , then, under  ℙ ^ p v , x \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x} , 
 

 
 
 ℒ ( ( U z ) z ∉ A ∪ D | K v W = A , R v = x ) = ⨂ z ∉ A ∪ D Unif [ 0 , 1 ] , \mathcal{L}\left((U_{z})_{z\notin A\cup D}\,\middle|\,K_{v}^{W}=A,\ R_{v}=x\right)=\bigotimes_{z\notin A\cup D}\operatorname{Unif}[0,1], 
 
 (5.36) 
 
 while the labels are independent uniform variables on  [ 0 , p ] [0,p]  for
 z ∈ A z\in A  and on  ( p , 1 ] (p,1]  for  z ∈ D z\in D . The same assertion holds
after deleting any set of edges determined by the layer field, with  D D 
interpreted as the tested boundary in that deterministic subgraph. 
 
 
 Here is the form needed when an allowed set is selected after an earlier
exploration. Let  ℱ old \mathcal{F}_{\rm old}  be the terminal sigma-field of
that exploration and let  Q old Q_{\rm old}  be its complete queried set.
Suppose that  W W  is  ℱ old \mathcal{F}_{\rm old} -measurable and that the
new cluster exploration queries only coordinates outside
 Q old Q_{\rm old} . If  A A  and  D D  are its newly queried open and closed
sets, respectively, then, in the finite-dimensional conditional-kernel
sense, 
 

 
 
 ℒ ( ( U z ) z ∉ Q old ∪ A ∪ D | ℱ old , A , D , R v = x ) = ⨂ z ∉ Q old ∪ A ∪ D Unif [ 0 , 1 ] . \mathcal{L}\left((U_{z})_{z\notin Q_{\rm old}\cup A\cup D}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{\rm old},A,D,R_{v}=x\right)=\bigotimes_{z\notin Q_{\rm old}\cup A\cup D}\operatorname{Unif}[0,1]. 
 
 (5.37) 
 
 Coordinates in  Q old Q_{\rm old}  retain the one-coordinate restrictions
recorded by  ℱ old \mathcal{F}_{\rm old} ; they do not become fresh merely
because the new allowed set omits them. If such coordinates are retained
as fixed boundary data, the same conclusion holds for the never-queried
coordinates, but no product-law assertion is made for the retained old
coordinates. 
 
 
 This is the stopped product kernel,
Lemma  2.1 , with the two-cell partition
 [ 0 , p ] ∪ ( p , 1 ] [0,p]\cup(p,1] . Here is the specialization, including the exact
terminal sigma-field used below. Fix a deterministic ordering of
the sites, use it to break every exploration tie, and let
 z 1 , z 2 , … z_{1},z_{2},\ldots  be the resulting sequence of queried sites. At the
moment  z i z_{i}  is selected, its identity is measurable with respect to
the previously queried labels and the layer field, whereas  U z i U_{z_{i}} 
is still uniform and independent of that information. For a finite
outcome  ( A , D ) (A,D) , the corresponding exploration atom is therefore 
 

 
 
 ⋂ z ∈ A { U z ≤ p } ∩ ⋂ z ∈ D { U z > p } , \bigcap_{z\in A}\{U_{z}\leq p\}\cap\bigcap_{z\in D}\{U_{z}>p\}, 
 
 
 together with conditions measurable with respect to the already fixed
layer field; no coordinate outside  A ∪ D A\cup D  occurs in this atom.
Independence of the labels gives ( 5.36 ) and the two truncated
uniform laws by direct factorization. The same induction applies when
there is an earlier query history, provided the entire query sequence is
kept: applying Lemma  2.1  to the concatenated
old and new queries gives ( 5.37 ), with
 Q old Q_{\rm old}  still excluded from the fresh-coordinate set. Notice
that predictability of the new queries alone would not permit one to
discard earlier queried coordinates. 
 
 
 If the exploration is infinite, let  Q n Q_{n}  be the set of its first
 n n  queried sites, let  Q ∞ = ⋃ n Q n Q_{\infty}=\bigcup_{n}Q_{n} , and let
 ℱ n \mathcal{F}_{n}  and  ℱ ∞ \mathcal{F}_{\infty}  be the corresponding finite
and terminal exploration sigma-fields. The precise stopping-set
identity is the following. For every deterministic finite set  S S ,
every bounded Borel function  g : [ 0 , 1 ] S → ℝ g:[0,1]^{S}\to\mathbb{R} , and every
bounded  ℱ ∞ \mathcal{F}_{\infty} -measurable random variable  H H , 
 

 
 
 𝔼 [ H 𝟏 { S ∩ Q ∞ = ∅ } g ( U S ) ] = 𝔼 [ H 𝟏 { S ∩ Q ∞ = ∅ } ] ∫ [ 0 , 1 ] S g ( u ) d u . \mathbb{E}[H\mathbf{1}_{\{S\cap Q_{\infty}=\varnothing\}}g(U_{S})]=\mathbb{E}[H\mathbf{1}_{\{S\cap Q_{\infty}=\varnothing\}}]\int_{[0,1]^{S}}g(u)\,du. 
 
 (5.38) 
 
 To verify ( 5.38 ), first take  H H  to be a cylinder function of
the first  n n  query choices and revealed labels and replace
 Q ∞ Q_{\infty}  by  Q m Q_{m} ,  m ≥ n m\geq n . Induction over queries gives the
identity because the next queried site is chosen before its label is
read. Letting  m → ∞ m\to\infty  gives the indicator in
( 5.38 ) by bounded convergence. Cylinder functions of the
exploration generate  ℱ ∞ \mathcal{F}_{\infty} , so a monotone-class argument
extends the identity to every bounded  H H . Identity ( 5.38 )
characterizes the conditional law of every finite subset of the
unqueried coordinates as independent uniform variables and hence gives
the residual product kernel. Thus later uses condition on the terminal
exploration sigma-field, not on a possibly zero-probability atom
specifying an infinite cluster. 
 
 
 Two-level boundary kernel.  If  p − < p + < 1 p_{-}<p_{+}<1  and the finite
 p − p_{-} -cluster is  A A , then, conditional on that cluster, the labels
on  ∂ V A \partial_{V}A  are independent uniform variables on  ( p − , 1 ] (p_{-},1] .
Consequently their  p + p_{+} -states are independent with opening
probability 
 

 
 
 ρ = p + − p − 1 − p − , \rho=\frac{p_{+}-p_{-}}{1-p_{-}}, 
 
 
 and every label outside  A ∪ ∂ V A A\cup\partial_{V}A  remains unconditioned.
Indeed, the cluster event is the intersection of
 { U z ≤ p − : z ∈ A } \{U_{z}\leq p_{-}:z\in A\}  and
 { U z > p − : z ∈ ∂ V A } \{U_{z}>p_{-}:z\in\partial_{V}A\} . Factoring this event coordinate by
coordinate gives the claimed boundary law, and for a boundary site  z z 
 

 
 
 ℙ ⁡ ( U z ≤ p + ∣ U z > p − ) = p + − p − 1 − p − = ρ . \mathbb{P}(U_{z}\leq p_{+}\mid U_{z}>p_{-})=\frac{p_{+}-p_{-}}{1-p_{-}}=\rho. 
 
 
 The factorization also proves the asserted mutual independence and shows
that no conditioning is imposed beyond  A ∪ ∂ V A A\cup\partial_{V}A . 
 
 
 First-hit residual kernel.  Fix a slab and expose the open-root
cluster only until its first visits to an extreme layer. Let
 w 1 , … , w N w_{1},\ldots,w_{N}  be a deterministic enumeration, from the exposed
data, of the open first-hit sites. Set  A 0 A_{0}  equal to the explored
part, and for  i ≥ 1 i\geq 1  explore the cluster of  w i w_{i}  in the remaining
allowed vertices after deleting all previously explored sites except
 w i w_{i} , retaining  w i w_{i}  as an open boundary root. Conditional on the
past before this
exploration,  U w i ≤ p U_{w_{i}}\leq p , every other untested label is uniform on
 [ 0 , 1 ] [0,1] , and some tested external-boundary labels may be forced into
 ( p , 1 ] (p,1] . Hence the residual cluster is stochastically dominated, for
every increasing cluster functional, by an independent cluster under
 ℙ ^ p w i \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{w_{i}} .
For the domination, couple the comparison cluster and the residual
cluster with the same uniforms on every untested non-root site. Give the
comparison cluster fresh uniforms on coordinates that the past has
already forced closed, while keeping those coordinates closed in the
residual exploration. Every open residual path then occurs in the
comparison configuration. The root coordinate has law
 Unif ⁡ [ 0 , p ] \operatorname{Unif}[0,p]  in both constructions and all remaining
comparison coordinates are unconditioned, so the comparison law is
exactly  ℙ ^ p w i \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{w_{i}} . 
 
 
 These identities specify the laws when a root or first-hit site has
already been declared open; there is no implicit endpoint convention and
no missing factor of  p p . They also exhaust the uses below. The peak
exploration in  H k ​ ( v ) H_{k}(v)  and the restricted-cluster exploration used in
the Reimer comparison both begin with  Q old = ∅ Q_{\rm old}=\varnothing , so
( 5.36 ) applies literally. The sequential increment explorations
have  Q old ≠ ∅ Q_{\rm old}\neq\varnothing ; there the preceding first-hit
residual coupling, rather than a fresh-law assertion for all sites outside
the new cluster, gives the required stochastic upper bound. 
 
 
 The exploration identities above specify every conditional law used in
what follows. In particular, ( 5.9 ) supplies the factor
 p − 1 p^{-1}  at each shared junction after the initial site is conditioned
open. 
 
 
 Layer probabilities and first moments.  Put 
 

 
 
 P ^ p ​ ( n ) = inf v , x ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( v ↔ L n ​ ( v ) ​  in  ​ L 0 , ∞ ​ ( v ) ) . \widehat{P}_{p}(n)=\inf_{v,x}\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}\bigl(v\leftrightarrow L_{n}(v)\text{ in }L_{0,\infty}(v)\bigr). 
 
 
 The site concatenation has the same one-layer buffer as ( 5.1 ): 
 

 
 
 P ^ p ​ ( m + n + 1 ) ≥ p ​ P ^ p ​ ( m ) ​ P ^ p ​ ( n ) ( m , n ≥ 0 ) . \widehat{P}_{p}(m+n+1)\geq p\,\widehat{P}_{p}(m)\widehat{P}_{p}(n)\qquad(m,n\geq 0). 
 
 (5.39) 
 
 Indeed, explore the first connection only to its first open site
 z ∈ L m ​ ( v ) z\in L_{m}(v) , without exploring from any top-layer site. Other sites of
 L m ​ ( v ) L_{m}(v)  may have been tested closed, but ( 5.7 ) puts
 z + z^{+}  in  L m + 1 ​ ( v ) L_{m+1}(v) , outside every tested coordinate. Conditional
on the exploration,  z + z^{+}  is open with probability  p p , and all sites
of  L 0 , ∞ ​ ( z + ) = L m + 1 , ∞ ​ ( v ) L_{0,\infty}(z^{+})=L_{m+1,\infty}(v) , apart from  z + z^{+} , retain
their product law. The conditional continuation probability to
 L n ​ ( z + ) = L m + n + 1 ​ ( v ) L_{n}(z^{+})=L_{m+n+1}(v)  is at least  P ^ p ​ ( n ) \widehat{P}_{p}(n) . This proves
( 5.39 ) and shows explicitly why the closed first-interface labels are
irrelevant. 
 
 
 Writing  a p ​ ( n ) = − log ⁡ P ^ p ​ ( n ) a_{p}(n)=-\log\widehat{P}_{p}(n)  and  c p = − log ⁡ p c_{p}=-\log p , the sequence
 a p ​ ( n − 1 ) + c p a_{p}(n-1)+c_{p} ,  n ≥ 1 n\geq 1 , is subadditive. Hence 
 

 
 
 lim n → ∞ a p ​ ( n ) t 0 ​ n = 1 t 0 ​ inf n ≥ 1 a p ​ ( n − 1 ) + c p n . \lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{a_{p}(n)}{t_{0}n}=\frac{1}{t_{0}}\inf_{n\geq 1}\frac{a_{p}(n-1)+c_{p}}{n}. 
 
 (5.40) 
 
 For every offset, the raw half-spaces and the layer half-spaces contain
one another after enlarging by at most one layer. Opening one
maximal-increment neighbor as in ( 5.7 ) handles the possible starting
layer. Consequently, uniformly in  v , x , n v,x,n , 
 

 
 
 A ^ p s ​ ( t 0 ​ ( n + 1 ) ) ≤ ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( v ↔ L n ​ ( v ) ​  in  ​ L 0 , ∞ ​ ( v ) ) ≤ p − 1 ​ A ^ p s ​ ( t 0 ​ n ) . \widehat{A}_{p}^{\rm s}(t_{0}(n+1))\leq\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{n}(v)\text{ in }L_{0,\infty}(v))\leq p^{-1}\widehat{A}_{p}^{\rm s}(t_{0}n). 
 
 (5.41) 
 
 To check the first inclusion, follow a path from raw height zero to height
 t 0 ​ ( n + 1 ) t_{0}(n+1) ; an edge changes height by at most  t 0 t_{0} , so the path
visits  L n ​ ( v ) L_{n}(v) . For the second, let  v − v^{-}  be a maximal-decrement
neighbor of  v v . Relative to  v − v^{-} , the entire set
 L 0 , ∞ ​ ( v ) L_{0,\infty}(v)  lies in raw height at least zero and  L n ​ ( v ) L_{n}(v)  lies
above raw height  t 0 ​ n t_{0}n . Let  E v E_{v}  be the crossing event in the
middle of ( 5.41 ) and let  G = { v − ​  open } G=\{v^{-}\text{ open}\} . Under the
law conditioned on  v v  being open, site FKG gives 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( E v ∩ G ) ≥ p ​ ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( E v ) . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(E_{v}\cap G)\geq p\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(E_{v}). 
 
 
 The event  E v ∩ G E_{v}\cap G  forces both  v v  and  v − v^{-}  open. Therefore
conditioning at either endpoint divides its unconditional probability
by the same factor  p p , and 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p v − , R v − ​ ( E v ∩ G ) = ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( E v ∩ G ) . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v^{-},R_{v^{-}}}(E_{v}\cap G)=\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(E_{v}\cap G). 
 
 
 On this event the edge  v − ​ v v^{-}v , followed by the  E v E_{v} -path, is a raw

 p ​ ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( E v ) ≤ A ^ p s ​ ( t 0 ​ n ) p\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(E_{v})\leq\widehat{A}_{p}^{\rm s}(t_{0}n) , which is the second inequality in
( 5.41 ).
Equations
( 5.3 ), ( 5.40 ), and ( 5.41 ) show that the limit in ( 5.40 ) is the
same  α p s \alpha_{p}^{\rm s}  as in ( 5.3 ), and ( 5.4 ) gives the uniform
probability-decay bound. All bounded connector and offset shifts below
change only multiplicative constants and not this rate. 
 
 
 We shall also use the following version with a lower slab enlargement:
for  n , r ≥ 0 n,r\geq 0 ,

 (5.42) 
 
 Indeed, let  u u  be obtained from  v v  by taking  r + 1 r+1  successive

 The connector factor can again be written without suppressing the

 The intersection forces both endpoints open, so its probabilities under

 Apply ( 5.4 ) and ( 5.41 ) to the crossing from  u u .
This proves ( 5.42 ) uniformly in the offset and uses only sites
on the displayed deterministic path. 
 
 
 For a first-visit decomposition at a site  z z ,

 For the second moment, on the event that
 u , w u,w  are both joined to  v v  in the slab, choose canonically a finite

Thus all fixed offsets have the same exponential rates, and Fekete’s

 Define first

 By transitivity these quantities do not depend on  v v . Apply ( 5.30 )
to 
 

 
 
 F ( u , z ) = 𝟏 { z ∈ L n ( u ) , u ↔ z  in the prescribed half-space } . F(u,z)=\mathbf{1}\{z\in L_{n}(u),\ u\leftrightarrow z\text{ in the prescribed half-space}\}. 
 
 
 If the reversed summand is nonzero, then  u ∈ L − n ​ ( z ) u\in L_{-n}(z) , and the
shift rule changes the path domain  L − ∞ , n ​ ( u ) L_{-\infty,n}(u)  into

 (5.49) 
 
 The fixed-offset comparisons ( 5.46 )–( 5.47 ) put every fixed-offset

rates in ( 5.49 ) therefore gives
 β p + = β p − \beta_{p}^{+}=\beta_{p}^{-} ; denote their common value by
 β p s \beta_{p}^{\rm s} . We record the one-layer comparison used next. Mark
a vertex  z z  counted by  X k − ∞ , k ​ ( v ) X_{k}^{-\infty,k}(v) , let  w = z + w=z^{+} , and
open  w w  if necessary. The image marked at  w w  is counted by
 X k + 1 − ∞ , k + 1 ​ ( v ) X_{k+1}^{-\infty,k+1}(v) . On the marked configuration space, this
map changes only the coordinate  w w . For a fixed image marked at
 w w , its former mark  z z  has at most  d d  choices and the original
state of  w w  has two choices. If  w w  was changed from closed to open,
the original-to-image Bernoulli weight ratio is  ( 1 − p ) / p (1-p)/p ; otherwise it
is one. Summing the fibres gives the explicit bound

 Equation ( 5.46 ) gives the reverse comparison after shifting
the index once. Applying the maximal-decrement map and
( 5.47 ) gives the analogous two-sided comparison for  D p D_{p} . 
 
 
 We also display how the offset averages are removed. Write
 U p sup , U p inf U_{p}^{\sup},U_{p}^{\inf}  and  D p sup , D p inf D_{p}^{\sup},D_{p}^{\inf}  for the
fixed-offset suprema and infima at the indicated index. Equation
( 5.46 ), its downward analogue, and averaging give 
 

 
 
 U p sup ​ ( k + 1 ) ≤ B p ​ U p inf ​ ( k ) ≤ B p ​ U ¯ p ​ ( k ) , D p sup ​ ( k + 1 ) ≤ B p ′ ​ D p inf ​ ( k ) ≤ B p ′ ​ D ¯ p ​ ( k ) . \begin{split}U_{p}^{\sup}(k+1)&\leq B_{p}U_{p}^{\inf}(k)\leq B_{p}\overline{U}_{p}(k),\\
D_{p}^{\sup}(k+1)&\leq B^{\prime}_{p}D_{p}^{\inf}(k)\leq B^{\prime}_{p}\overline{D}_{p}(k).\end{split} 
 
 
 The one-layer finite-energy maps compare either supremum at indices
differing by a bounded amount. Therefore, using
 U ¯ p ​ ( j ) ≤ U p sup ​ ( j ) \overline{U}_{p}(j)\leq U_{p}^{\sup}(j) ,
 D ¯ p ​ ( j ) ≤ D p sup ​ ( j ) \overline{D}_{p}(j)\leq D_{p}^{\sup}(j) , and
( 5.49 ), we obtain, for example, 
 

 
 
 D p sup ​ ( k ) ≤ B p ′ ​ D ¯ p ​ ( k − 1 ) ≤ C p ​ e t 0 ​ ( k − 1 ) ​ U ¯ p ​ ( k − 1 ) ≤ C p ​ e t 0 ​ k ​ U p sup ​ ( k + 1 ) . \begin{split}D_{p}^{\sup}(k)&\leq B^{\prime}_{p}\overline{D}_{p}(k-1)\leq C_{p}e^{t_{0}(k-1)}\overline{U}_{p}(k-1)\\
&\leq C_{p}e^{t_{0}k}U_{p}^{\sup}(k+1).\end{split} 
 
 
 The same chain with  U , D U,D  interchanged yields the reverse relation.
Since  U p = U p sup U_{p}=U_{p}^{\sup}  and  D p = D p sup D_{p}=D_{p}^{\sup}  by definition, this
proves, for a constant  C p C_{p} , 
 

 
 
 D p ​ ( k ) ≤ C p ​ e t 0 ​ k ​ U p ​ ( k + 1 ) , U p ​ ( k ) ≤ C p ​ e − t 0 ​ k ​ D p ​ ( k + 1 ) . D_{p}(k)\leq C_{p}e^{t_{0}k}U_{p}(k+1),\qquad U_{p}(k)\leq C_{p}e^{-t_{0}k}D_{p}(k+1). 
 
 (5.50) 
 
 Substituting ( 5.48 ) into ( 5.50 ) and absorbing the fixed terms
 t 0 ​ β p s t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}  and  t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)  into the constant
gives 
 

 
 
 h p ′ ​ ( k ) ≤ h p ​ ( k + 1 ) + C p , h p ​ ( k ) ≤ h p ′ ​ ( k + 1 ) + C p , h^{\prime}_{p}(k)\leq h_{p}(k+1)+C_{p},\qquad h_{p}(k)\leq h^{\prime}_{p}(k+1)+C_{p}, 
 
 
 and hence the two limits in ( 5.32 ) agree with uniform error terms.
More precisely, the connector comparison gives 
 
 
 
 
 b p − 1 ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k + h p ​ ( k + 1 ) \displaystyle b_{p}^{-1}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k+h_{p}(k+1)} 
 ≤ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ X k − ∞ , k ​ ( v ) ≤ b p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k + h p ​ ( k ) , \displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}X_{k}^{-\infty,k}(v)\leq b_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k+h_{p}(k)}, 
 
 (5.51) 
 
 
 b p − 1 ​ e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ k + h p ′ ​ ( k + 1 ) \displaystyle b_{p}^{-1}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)k+h^{\prime}_{p}(k+1)} 
 ≤ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ X − k − ∞ , 0 ​ ( v ) ≤ b p ​ e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ k + h p ′ ​ ( k ) . \displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}X_{-k}^{-\infty,0}(v)\leq b_{p}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)k+h^{\prime}_{p}(k)}. 
 
 
 The existence of the separate limits followed from Fekete’s lemma, their
equality from ( 5.49 ), and their uniformity in the root and offset from
( 5.46 )–( 5.47 ). This proves clause 1. 
 
 
 Suppose  β p s > 1 / 2 \beta_{p}^{\rm s}>1/2 . Apply the upper extreme-visit inequality
and ( 5.51 ). For  k ≥ 0 k\geq 0 , write its extreme layer as  j = k + r j=k+r ,
where  r ≥ 0 r\geq 0 . The upward factor in the summand is at most 
 

 
 
 C p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ ( k + r ) + h p ​ ( k + r ) , C_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}(k+r)+h_{p}(k+r)}, 
 
 
 while the downward factor from layer  j j  to layer  k k  is at most 
 

 
 
 C p ​ e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ r + h p ′ ​ ( r ) . C_{p}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)r+h^{\prime}_{p}(r)}. 
 
 
 Subadditivity gives  h p ​ ( k + r ) ≤ h p ​ ( k ) + h p ​ ( r ) h_{p}(k+r)\leq h_{p}(k)+h_{p}(r) . Factoring out the
terms depending only on  k k  therefore gives 
 

 
 
 E ^ p − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( k ) ≤ C p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k + h p ​ ( k ) ​ ∑ r ≥ 0 e − t 0 ​ ( 2 ​ β p s − 1 ) ​ r + h p ​ ( r ) + h p ′ ​ ( r ) . \widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,\infty}(k)\leq C_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k+h_{p}(k)}\sum_{r\geq 0}e^{-t_{0}(2\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)r+h_{p}(r)+h^{\prime}_{p}(r)}. 
 
 (5.52) 
 
 The series is finite because  h p ​ ( r ) + h p ′ ​ ( r ) = o ⁡ ( r ) h_{p}(r)+h^{\prime}_{p}(r)=o(r) . For negative
targets, a reflected minimum-layer decomposition is not used, since it
would constrain its two path pieces below rather than above. Instead
fix  q ≥ 0 q\geq 0  and decompose a path from layer  0 0  to layer  − q -q  at
its maximum layer  j ≥ 0 j\geq 0 . Applying the third inequality in
( 5.43 ), first with a finite upper cutoff and then using
monotone convergence, gives 
 

 
 
 E ^ p − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( − q ) ≤ ∑ j ≥ 0 U p ​ ( j ) ​ D p ​ ( q + j ) . \widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,\infty}(-q)\leq\sum_{j\geq 0}U_{p}(j)D_{p}(q+j). 
 
 
 The first factor counts the initial path up to its first visit to its
maximum layer; after shifting by  j j , the second factor counts the
remaining path to layer  − q − j -q-j  in an upper-bounded half-space. Thus
both factors are exactly among those estimated in ( 5.51 ).
Using that estimate and
 h p ′ ​ ( q + j ) ≤ h p ′ ​ ( q ) + h p ′ ​ ( j ) h^{\prime}_{p}(q+j)\leq h^{\prime}_{p}(q)+h^{\prime}_{p}(j) , we obtain 
 

 
 
 E ^ p − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( − q ) ≤ C p ​ e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ q + h p ′ ​ ( q ) ​ ∑ j ≥ 0 e − t 0 ​ ( 2 ​ β p s − 1 ) ​ j + h p ​ ( j ) + h p ′ ​ ( j ) . \widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,\infty}(-q)\leq C_{p}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)q+h^{\prime}_{p}(q)}\sum_{j\geq 0}e^{-t_{0}(2\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)j+h_{p}(j)+h^{\prime}_{p}(j)}. 
 
 (5.53) 
 
 This is the same convergent series as in ( 5.52 ); no
height-reflection symmetry or lower-bounded half-space estimate is
being assumed. Restricting the full cluster to
 L − ∞ , k L_{-\infty,k}  for  k ≥ 0 k\geq 0 , or to  L − ∞ , 0 L_{-\infty,0}  for
 k < 0 k<0 , gives the matching lower bounds directly from ( 5.51 ). Hence 
 

 
 
 c p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k ≤ E ^ p − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( k ) ≤ C p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k + h p ​ ( k ) , k ≥ 0 , c p ​ e t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ k ≤ E ^ p − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( k ) ≤ C p ​ e t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ k + h p ′ ​ ( − k ) , k < 0 . \begin{array}[]{ll}c_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k}\leq\widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,\infty}(k)\leq C_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k+h_{p}(k)},&k\geq 0,\\[5.69054pt]
c_{p}e^{t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)k}\leq\widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,\infty}(k)\leq C_{p}e^{t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)k+h^{\prime}_{p}(-k)},&k<0.\end{array} 
 
 (5.54) 
 
 
 
 We now show that the error functions are bounded when
 p < p t s p<p_{t}^{\rm s} . Indeed,
 χ ^ p , 1 / 2 < ∞ \widehat{\chi}_{p,1/2}<\infty  first implies  p ∈ I h s p\in I_{h}^{\rm s} .
If  β p s ≤ 1 / 2 \beta_{p}^{\rm s}\leq 1/2 , the lower bounds in ( 5.51 ), multiplied
by the layer weights  e t 0 ​ k / 2 e^{t_{0}k/2}  and summed over  k k , would make
 χ ^ p , 1 / 2 \widehat{\chi}_{p,1/2}  infinite. Hence
 β p s > 1 / 2 \beta_{p}^{\rm s}>1/2 ; conversely
( 5.52 ) and ( 5.53 ) show
that this strict inequality makes the half-tilted susceptibility finite.
Apply ( 5.45 ) with  m = 0 m=0 ,  n = k n=k , and both target layers equal to
 k k . Reindex the branch layer as  k − ℓ k-\ell , dominate every restricted
first moment by the full-cluster bound ( 5.54 ), and obtain 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( X k 0 , k ) 2 ] ≤ C p ​ ∑ ℓ = 0 k e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ ( k − ℓ ) + h p ​ ( k − ℓ ) ​ e − 2 ​ t 0 ​ β p s ​ ℓ + 2 ​ h p ​ ( ℓ ) ≤ C p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k + M k ​ ∑ ℓ ≥ 0 e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ ℓ + 2 ​ h p ​ ( ℓ ) , \begin{split}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[(X_{k}^{0,k})^{2}]&\leq C_{p}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}(k-\ell)+h_{p}(k-\ell)}e^{-2t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}\ell+2h_{p}(\ell)}\\
&\leq C_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k+M_{k}}\sum_{\ell\geq 0}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}\ell+2h_{p}(\ell)},\end{split} 
 
 
 where  M k = max 0 ≤ j ≤ k ⁡ h p ​ ( j ) M_{k}=\max_{0\leq j\leq k}h_{p}(j) . The last series is finite
because  h p ​ ( ℓ ) = o ​ ( ℓ ) h_{p}(\ell)=o(\ell)  and  β p s > 1 / 2 \beta_{p}^{\rm s}>1/2 . Absorbing it
into the constant gives

 (5.55) 
 
 The last-visit inequality in ( 5.43 ), used with
 ( m , n , ℓ ) = ( − ∞ , k , 0 ) (m,n,\ell)=(-\infty,k,0) , gives 
 

 
 
 E ^ p − ∞ , k ​ ( k ) ≤ E ^ p − ∞ , k ​ ( 0 ) ​ E ^ p 0 , k ​ ( k ) ≤ E ^ p − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( 0 ) ​ E ^ p 0 , k ​ ( k ) . \widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,k}(k)\leq\widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,k}(0)\widehat{E}_{p}^{0,k}(k)\leq\widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,\infty}(0)\widehat{E}_{p}^{0,k}(k). 
 
 
 The zero-layer factor is finite for  p < p t s p<p_{t}^{\rm s} . Rearranging and
using ( 5.51 ) gives

 This is a supremal estimate; no pointwise lower bound for the slab first

 ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( X k 0 , k > 0 ) ≥ ( 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ X k 0 , k ) 2 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( X k 0 , k ) 2 ] . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(X_{k}^{0,k}>0)\geq\frac{(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}X_{k}^{0,k})^{2}}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[(X_{k}^{0,k})^{2}]}. 
 
 
 The denominator has the uniform upper bound ( 5.55 ). Since the
supremum in ( 5.56 ) is positive, choose  ( v , x ) (v,x)  whose first
moment is at least one half of that supremum and apply the preceding
inequality. This gives the supremal crossing bound 
 

 
 
 sup v , x ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( v ↔ L k ​ ( v ) ​  in  ​ L 0 , k ​ ( v ) ) ≥ c p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k + 2 ​ h p ​ ( k ) − max j ≤ k ⁡ h p ​ ( j ) . \sup_{v,x}\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{k}(v)\text{ in }L_{0,k}(v))\geq c_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k+2h_{p}(k)-\max_{j\leq k}h_{p}(j)}. 
 
 (5.57) 
 
 
 
 We justify the upper bound used to control  h p h_{p}  without assuming the
conclusion  α p s = β p s \alpha_{p}^{\rm s}=\beta_{p}^{\rm s} . For every  v , x v,x , 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( v ↔ L k ​ ( v ) ​  in  ​ L 0 , ∞ ​ ( v ) ) ≤ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ X k 0 , k ​ ( v ) ≤ U p ​ ( k ) = e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k + h p ​ ( k ) . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{k}(v)\text{ in }L_{0,\infty}(v))\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}X_{k}^{0,k}(v)\leq U_{p}(k)=e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k+h_{p}(k)}. 
 
 
 Equation ( 5.41 ) shows that the probability on the left has
exponential decay rate  α p s \alpha_{p}^{\rm s} , uniformly in  v , x v,x , while
 h p ​ ( k ) = o ​ ( k ) h_{p}(k)=o(k) . It follows that

( 5.4 ) and the upper inequality in ( 5.41 ) give

 (5.58) 
 
 Reaching  L k ​ ( v ) L_{k}(v)  inside  L 0 , ∞ ​ ( v ) L_{0,\infty}(v)  is equivalent to doing so
inside  L 0 , k ​ ( v ) L_{0,k}(v) , by stopping a witnessing path at its first visit to
 L k ​ ( v ) L_{k}(v) . Put  M k = max 0 ≤ j ≤ k ⁡ h p ​ ( j ) M_{k}=\max_{0\leq j\leq k}h_{p}(j) . Comparing
( 5.57 ) with ( 5.58 ) gives 
 

 
 
 2 ​ h p ​ ( k ) − M k ≤ C p . 2h_{p}(k)-M_{k}\leq C_{p}. 
 
 
 If  k k  is a record time, then  h p ​ ( k ) = M k h_{p}(k)=M_{k} , and hence

 sup k h p ​ ( k ) < ∞ \sup_{k}h_{p}(k)<\infty .
Substituting the definitions in ( 5.48 ) into the first
one-layer comparison in ( 5.50 ) then gives
 sup k h p ′ ​ ( k ) < ∞ \sup_{k}h^{\prime}_{p}(k)<\infty . 
 
 
 It remains to recover the pointwise lower bound, rather than silently
promote the supremum in ( 5.57 ). Since  h p h_{p}  is now bounded,
that equation and the upper inequality in ( 5.41 ) imply, for
 k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , 
 

 
 
 A ^ p s ​ ( t 0 ​ k ) ≥ p ​ sup v , x ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( v ↔ L k ​ ( v ) ​  in  ​ L 0 , ∞ ​ ( v ) ) ≥ c p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k . \widehat{A}_{p}^{\rm s}(t_{0}k)\geq p\sup_{v,x}\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{k}(v)\text{ in }L_{0,\infty}(v))\geq c_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k}. 
 
 
 Together with ( 5.4 ) and the already proved inequality
 α p s ≥ β p s \alpha_{p}^{\rm s}\geq\beta_{p}^{\rm s} , this proves

( 5.41 ), now used at every fixed  v , x v,x , yields 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( v ↔ L k ​ ( v ) ​  in  ​ L 0 , ∞ ​ ( v ) ) ≥ A ^ p s ​ ( t 0 ​ ( k + 1 ) ) ≥ c p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{k}(v)\text{ in }L_{0,\infty}(v))\geq\widehat{A}_{p}^{\rm s}(t_{0}(k+1))\geq c_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k}. 
 
 
 The matching upper bound is ( 5.58 ). The unrestricted
probability has the same lower bound, while Markov’s inequality and the
bounded-error form of ( 5.54 ) give its upper bound.
Thus, uniformly in  v , x , k v,x,k , 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p v , x ( v ↔ L k ( v )  in  L 0 , ∞ ( v ) ) ≍ p exp [ − t 0 β p s k ] \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{k}(v)\text{ in }L_{0,\infty}(v))\asymp_{p}\exp[-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k] 
 
 (5.59) 
 
 and the same estimate without the half-space restriction. Comparing with
the definition of  α p s \alpha_{p}^{\rm s}  is consistent with the equality
proved above. For every
 p ∈ I h s p\in I_{h}^{\rm s}  with  p < p t s p<p_{t}^{\rm s} , summing the now
bounded-error form of ( 5.54 ) over all layers at tilt  0 0  gives 
 

 
 
 χ ^ p , 0 ≍ ∑ k ≥ 0 p ⁡ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k + ∑ r ≥ 1 e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ r . \widehat{\chi}_{p,0}\asymp_{p}\sum_{k\geq 0}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k}+\sum_{r\geq 1}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)r}. 
 
 
 The first series converges since  β p s > 1 / 2 \beta_{p}^{\rm s}>1/2 , and the second
converges exactly when  β p s > 1 \beta_{p}^{\rm s}>1 . Site sharpness
 [ 2 , Theorem 2 and Section 6]  identifies finiteness of the ordinary
susceptibility with  p < p c s p<p_{c}^{\rm s} .
Consequently 
 

 
 
 p < p c s ⟺ β p s > 1 . p<p_{c}^{\rm s}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\beta_{p}^{\rm s}>1. 
 
 (5.60) 
 
 
 
 We make the branch-layer summation used for peaks explicit. If
 p < p c s p<p_{c}^{\rm s} , write  β = β p s > 1 \beta=\beta_{p}^{\rm s}>1  and 
 

 
 
 φ β ​ ( j ) = { e − t 0 ​ β ​ j , j ≥ 0 , e t 0 ​ ( β − 1 ) ​ j , j < 0 . \varphi_{\beta}(j)=\begin{cases}e^{-t_{0}\beta j},&j\geq 0,\\
e^{t_{0}(\beta-1)j},&j<0.\end{cases} 
 
 
 The bounded-error form of ( 5.54 ) says
 E ^ p − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( j ) ≤ C p ​ φ β ​ ( j ) \widehat{E}_{p}^{-\infty,\infty}(j)\leq C_{p}\varphi_{\beta}(j) .
Substitution in ( 5.45 ) gives 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ X − k − ∞ , ∞ ​ X − ℓ − ∞ , ∞ ] ≤ C p ​ ∑ j ∈ ℤ φ β ​ ( j ) ​ φ β ​ ( − k − j ) ​ φ β ​ ( − ℓ − j ) . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[X_{-k}^{-\infty,\infty}X_{-\ell}^{-\infty,\infty}]\leq C_{p}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varphi_{\beta}(j)\varphi_{\beta}(-k-j)\varphi_{\beta}(-\ell-j). 
 
 
 Assume without loss of generality that  0 ≤ k ≤ ℓ 0\leq k\leq\ell . Split the
sum at  − ℓ , − k , 0 -\ell,-k,0 . Writing
 t 0 − 1 ​ log ⁡ [ φ β ​ ( j ) ​ φ β ​ ( − k − j ) ​ φ β ​ ( − ℓ − j ) ] t_{0}^{-1}\log[\varphi_{\beta}(j)\varphi_{\beta}(-k-j)\varphi_{\beta}(-\ell-j)]  as  E ⁡ ( j ) E(j) , direct substitution gives 
 

 
 
 E ⁡ ( j ) = { β ⁡ ( k + ℓ ) + ( 3 ​ β − 1 ) ​ j , j ≤ − ℓ , β ⁡ ( k + j ) − ( β − 1 ) ​ ℓ , − ℓ ≤ j ≤ − k , − ( β − 1 ) ​ ( k + ℓ + j ) , − k ≤ j ≤ 0 , − ( β − 1 ) ​ ( k + ℓ ) − ( 3 ​ β − 2 ) ​ j , j ≥ 0 . E(j)=\begin{cases}\beta(k+\ell)+(3\beta-1)j,&j\leq-\ell,\\
\beta(k+j)-(\beta-1)\ell,&-\ell\leq j\leq-k,\\
-(\beta-1)(k+\ell+j),&-k\leq j\leq 0,\\
-(\beta-1)(k+\ell)-(3\beta-2)j,&j\geq 0.\end{cases} 
 
 
 At  j = − ℓ j=-\ell , the first expression is at most
 − ( β − 1 ) ​ ℓ -(\beta-1)\ell  because  k ≤ ℓ k\leq\ell , and it decreases at rate
 3 ​ β − 1 3\beta-1  as  j j  moves left. The second expression is maximized at
 j = − k j=-k , where it equals  − ( β − 1 ) ​ ℓ -(\beta-1)\ell . The third is maximized at
 j = − k j=-k , with the same value, and the fourth is at most
 − ( β − 1 ) ​ ℓ -(\beta-1)\ell  at zero and decreases at rate  3 ​ β − 2 3\beta-2 .
All four rates are positive because  β > 1 \beta>1 . Thus each range is a
geometric sum bounded by a constant times
 e − t 0 ​ ( β − 1 ) ​ ℓ e^{-t_{0}(\beta-1)\ell} , uniformly in  k , ℓ k,\ell , and hence 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ X − k − ∞ , ∞ ​ X − ℓ − ∞ , ∞ ] ≤ C p ​ e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ ( k ∨ ℓ ) . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[X_{-k}^{-\infty,\infty}X_{-\ell}^{-\infty,\infty}]\leq C_{p}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)(k\vee\ell)}. 
 
 (5.61) 
 
 For the upward count, use ( 5.45 ) with  m = 0 m=0 ,  n = k n=k , and both target
layers equal to  k k , and dominate each restricted first moment by its
full-cluster counterpart. This gives 
 
 
 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( X k 0 , k ) 2 ] \displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[(X_{k}^{0,k})^{2}] 
 ≤ C p ​ ∑ j = 0 k e − t 0 ​ β ​ j ​ e − 2 ​ t 0 ​ β ​ ( k − j ) \displaystyle\leq C_{p}\sum_{j=0}^{k}e^{-t_{0}\beta j}e^{-2t_{0}\beta(k-j)} 
 
 (5.62) 
 
 
 
 ≤ C p ​ e − t 0 ​ β ​ k . \displaystyle\leq C_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta k}. 
 
 
 All geometric ratios depend only on  G , p G,p . Thus the constants in
( 5.61 )–( 5.62 ) are uniform in  v , x , k , ℓ v,x,k,\ell . 
 
 
 Continue to assume that  p < p c s p<p_{c}^{\rm s} . We spell out the peak
consequences. A vertex  z z  is the peak of a set
 A ∋ z A\ni z  when  log ⁡ Δ ⁡ ( z , u ) < 0 \log\Delta(z,u)<0  for all  u ∈ A ∖ { z } u\in A\setminus\{z\} .
Let  𝒫 z \mathcal{P}_{z}  be the event that  z z  is the peak of its full open
cluster. Let  H k ​ ( v ) H_{k}(v)  be the graph on  L − ∞ , k ​ ( v ) L_{-\infty,k}(v)  obtained by
deleting every edge with both endpoints in  L k ​ ( v ) L_{k}(v) . Explore the open
cluster  K k ​ ( v ) K_{k}(v)  of  v v  in  H k ​ ( v ) H_{k}(v) , and put
 Z k = K k ​ ( v ) ∩ L k ​ ( v ) Z_{k}=K_{k}(v)\cap L_{k}(v) . Thus a site adjacent through a deleted
top-layer edge, or lying strictly above the top layer, has not been
tested merely because  K k ​ ( v ) K_{k}(v)  was exposed. Moreover
 Z k ≠ ∅ Z_{k}\neq\varnothing  contains the first-hit event used in the upward
half-space crossing estimate. Moreover,
 | Z k | ≤ X k − ∞ , k ​ ( v ) |Z_{k}|\leq X_{k}^{-\infty,k}(v) . Since  h p h_{p}  is bounded, the upper
inequality in ( 5.51 ) and the pointwise lower bound
( 5.59 ) give, uniformly in  v , x , k v,x,k , 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ | Z k | ≤ C p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k , ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( Z k ≠ ∅ ) ≥ c p ​ e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}|Z_{k}|\leq C_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k},\qquad\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(Z_{k}\neq\varnothing)\geq c_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k}. 
 
 
 Dividing the first estimate by the second proves 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ ( | Z k | ∣ Z k ≠ ∅ ) ≤ C p . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}(|Z_{k}|\mid Z_{k}\neq\varnothing)\leq C_{p}. 
 
 (5.63) 
 
 On  Z k ≠ ∅ Z_{k}\neq\varnothing , select  u ∈ Z k u\in Z_{k}  measurably from the
exploration and let  u + u^{+}  be a maximal-increment neighbor from ( 5.7 ).
Then  u + ∈ L k + 1 ​ ( v ) u^{+}\in L_{k+1}(v) . More explicitly, writing
 q = log ⁡ Δ ⁡ ( v , u ) / t 0 q=\log\Delta(v,u)/t_{0} , the inequalities
 k + R v − 1 < q ≤ k + R v k+R_{v}-1<q\leq k+R_{v}  imply
 k + R v < q + 1 ≤ k + R v + 1 k+R_{v}<q+1\leq k+R_{v}+1 , which is precisely membership in
 L k + 1 ​ ( v ) L_{k+1}(v) . Thus  u + ∉ L − ∞ , k ​ ( v ) u^{+}\notin L_{-\infty,k}(v) , and the exploration
of  K k ​ ( v ) K_{k}(v)  has not queried  U u + U_{u^{+}} . 
 
 
 Force  u + u^{+}  open and close every neighbor of
 Z k ∪ { u + } Z_{k}\cup\{u^{+}\}  outside  K k ​ ( v ) ∪ { u + } K_{k}(v)\cup\{u^{+}\} . A site in this closing
set that was queried during the exploration is already closed: every
queried open site joined through a retained edge belongs to  K k ​ ( v ) K_{k}(v) ,
while the deleted top-layer edges and every site above layer  k k  were
not queried through the exploration frontier. Consequently the only
open coordinate prescribed by the surgery is the genuinely untested
site  u + u^{+} , and every newly prescribed closed coordinate is also
untested. There are at most  d ⁡ ( | Z k | + 1 ) d(|Z_{k}|+1)  of the latter. Conditional on
the exploration, ( 5.36 ) therefore gives probability at least 
 

 
 
 p ​ ( 1 − p ) d ⁡ ( | Z k | + 1 ) . p(1-p)^{d(|Z_{k}|+1)}. 
 
 
 Every edge from  K k ​ ( v ) K_{k}(v)  to an untested site is incident to  Z k Z_{k} :
an edge cannot increase raw height by more than  t 0 t_{0} , and all retained
frontier sites were tested. Hence on this surgery event the full cluster
is exactly  K k ​ ( v ) ∪ { u + } K_{k}(v)\cup\{u^{+}\} . Since every vertex of  K k ​ ( v ) K_{k}(v)  is in
 L − ∞ , k ​ ( v ) L_{-\infty,k}(v)  and the strict lower endpoint of  L k ​ ( v ) L_{k}(v)  implies
 log ⁡ Δ ⁡ ( v , u + ) > t 0 ​ ( k + R v ) \log\Delta(v,u^{+})>t_{0}(k+R_{v}) , the vertex  u + u^{+}  is its unique peak.
We now record the probability calculation that turns this surgery into a
uniform lower bound. Put  a = 1 − p a=1-p  and
 H k = { Z k ≠ ∅ } H_{k}=\{Z_{k}\neq\varnothing\} . Since
 s ↦ a d ​ s = exp ⁡ ( d ​ s ​ log ⁡ a ) s\mapsto a^{ds}=\exp(ds\log a)  is convex and decreasing,
conditional Jensen and ( 5.63 ) give 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ p ​ a d ⁡ ( | Z k | + 1 ) ∣ H k ] = p ​ a d ​ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ a d ​ | Z k | ∣ H k ] ≥ p ​ a d ​ a d ​ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ ( | Z k | ∣ H k ) ≥ p ​ a d ⁡ ( C p + 1 ) = : c p ′ > 0 . \begin{split}&\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[pa^{d(|Z_{k}|+1)}\mid H_{k}]\\
&\qquad=pa^{d}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[a^{d|Z_{k}|}\mid H_{k}]\geq pa^{d}a^{d\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}(|Z_{k}|\mid H_{k})}\geq pa^{d(C_{p}+1)}=:c_{p}^{\prime}>0.\end{split} 
 
 (5.64) 
 
 The event  H k H_{k}  is unchanged by deleting edges internal to the
first-hit layer and hence is precisely the unrestricted upward hitting
event. The unrestricted version of ( 5.59 )
therefore gives 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p v , x ( H k ) ≍ p e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(H_{k})\asymp_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k}. 
 
 
 Integrating the conditional surgery estimate gives the same expression
as a lower bound for the peak event in the next layer. Conversely, if
the peak of  K v K_{v}  lies in  L k + 1 ​ ( v ) L_{k+1}(v) , then  v v  is connected to
that layer; the unrestricted upward hitting estimate, with  k + 1 k+1  in
place of  k k , gives the matching upper bound. Absorbing the fixed
one-layer factor into the constants proves 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p v , x ( peak ( K v ) ∈ L k + 1 ( v ) ) ≍ p e − t 0 ​ β p s ​ k . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}\left(\operatorname{peak}(K_{v})\in L_{k+1}(v)\right)\asymp_{p}e^{-t_{0}\beta_{p}^{\rm s}k}. 
 
 (5.65) 
 
 
 
 Auxiliary Lemma 5.G   (Peak-preserving offset surgery) . 
 
 For
 0 < p ≤ p c s 0<p\leq p_{c}^{\rm s} , set 
 

 
 
 F j ​ ( v , x ) = 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ X j − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( v ) ; 𝒫 v ] . F_{j}(v,x)=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[X_{j}^{-\infty,\infty}(v);\mathcal{P}_{v}]. 
 
 
 There is  C = C ⁡ ( d , p ) < ∞ C=C(d,p)<\infty  such that, for every  j ≤ − 1 j\leq-1 , 
 

 
 
 F j − 1 ​ ( v , x ) ≤ C ​ F j ​ ( v , x ) , F j + 1 ​ ( v , x ) ≤ C ​ F j ​ ( v , x ) . F_{j-1}(v,x)\leq CF_{j}(v,x),\qquad F_{j+1}(v,x)\leq CF_{j}(v,x). 
 
 (5.66) 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Equip each configuration on the left with a
marked vertex
 z ∈ K v ∩ L j − 1 ​ ( v ) z\in K_{v}\cap L_{j-1}(v) , respectively
 z ∈ K v ∩ L j + 1 ​ ( v ) z\in K_{v}\cap L_{j+1}(v) , on the event  𝒫 v \mathcal{P}_{v} .
Let  w = z + ∈ L j ​ ( v ) w=z^{+}\in L_{j}(v) , respectively  w = z − ∈ L j ​ ( v ) w=z^{-}\in L_{j}(v) , where
 z + z^{+}  and  z − z^{-}  are maximal-increment and maximal-decrement
neighbors. If  w ∈ K v w\in K_{v} , leave the configuration unchanged. Otherwise
 w w  is closed, since it is adjacent to  z ∈ K v z\in K_{v} . Open  w w  and
close every vertex in 
 

 
 
 N ⁡ ( w ) ∖ ( K v ∪ { w } ) . N(w)\setminus(K_{v}\cup\{w\}). 
 
 
 The resulting root cluster is exactly  K v ∪ { w } K_{v}\cup\{w\} . Since
 j ≤ − 1 j\leq-1  and  x < 1 x<1 , every  w ∈ L j x ​ ( v ) w\in L_{j}^{x}(v)  satisfies 
 

 
 
 log ⁡ Δ ⁡ ( v , w ) ≤ t 0 ​ ( j + x ) < 0 . \log\Delta(v,w)\leq t_{0}(j+x)<0. 
 
 
 Thus  v v  remains the unique peak of its full cluster, and  w w  is
counted by  X j − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( v ) X_{j}^{-\infty,\infty}(v) . 
 
 
 This defines a map from the marked configurations counted by the
left-hand side of either inequality in ( 5.66 ) to the marked
configurations  ( ω ′ , w ) (\omega^{\prime},w)  counted by  F j ​ ( v , x ) F_{j}(v,x) . It changes at
most  d + 1 d+1  non-root coordinates. For a fixed image marked at  w w ,
there are at most  d d  choices of its marked neighbor  z z  and at most
 2 d + 1 2^{d+1}  choices for the original states in
 N ⁡ ( w ) ∪ { w } N(w)\cup\{w\} . Under the conditional product measure given that
 v v  is open, comparison of Bernoulli weights therefore bounds the
measure of all marked preimages by 
 

 
 
 d ​  2 d + 1 ​ [ p ⁡ ( 1 − p ) ] − ( d + 1 ) d\,2^{d+1}[p(1-p)]^{-(d+1)} 
 
 
 times the measure of marked images. Summing over the marked vertex proves
( 5.66 ). Full clusters are finite in this parameter range: this follows
from the definition of  p c s p_{c}^{\rm s}  below criticality and from the
Auxiliary Lemma  5.F  at equality. Hence the map is
defined almost
surely.
∎ 
 
 
 
 For arbitrary offsets  x , y ∈ [ 0 , 1 ) x,y\in[0,1) , the half-open layer definition
gives 
 

 
 
 L j x ​ ( v ) ⊆ L j − 1 , j + 1 y ​ ( v ) . L_{j}^{x}(v)\subseteq L_{j-1,j+1}^{y}(v). 
 
 
 The event  𝒫 v \mathcal{P}_{v}  depends only on the modular cocycle and the
site configuration, not on the auxiliary offset. Therefore ( 5.66 )
and the preceding inclusion give, with  C 0 C_{0}  denoting the constant in
( 5.66 ), 
 

 
 
 F j ​ ( v , x ) ≤ F j − 1 ​ ( v , y ) + F j ​ ( v , y ) + F j + 1 ​ ( v , y ) ≤ ( 2 ​ C 0 + 1 ) ​ F j ​ ( v , y ) . \begin{split}F_{j}(v,x)&\leq F_{j-1}(v,y)+F_{j}(v,y)+F_{j+1}(v,y)\\
&\leq(2C_{0}+1)F_{j}(v,y).\end{split} 
 
 
 Interchanging  x x  and  y y  gives the reverse inequality. Thus, after
writing  C = 2 ​ C 0 + 1 C=2C_{0}+1 , 
 

 
 
 C − 1 ​ F j ​ ( v , x ) ≤ F j ​ ( v , y ) ≤ C ​ F j ​ ( v , x ) ( j ≤ − 1 ) . C^{-1}F_{j}(v,x)\leq F_{j}(v,y)\leq CF_{j}(v,x)\qquad(j\leq-1). 
 
 (5.67) 
 
 Integrating the two inequalities in  y y  displays the comparison with
the offset average: 
 

 
 
 C − 1 ​ F j ​ ( v , x ) ≤ ∫ 0 1 F j ​ ( v , y ) ​ 𝑑 y ≤ C ​ F j ​ ( v , x ) . C^{-1}F_{j}(v,x)\leq\int_{0}^{1}F_{j}(v,y)\,dy\leq CF_{j}(v,x). 
 
 
 This two-sided comparison
comes from the peak-preserving finite-energy map
( 5.66 ), not from the one-sided slab comparison
( 5.107 ); in particular the peak event is retained throughout.
For  j = 0 j=0 , the same conclusion follows from 
 

 
 
 ( 1 − p ) d ≤ F 0 ​ ( v , x ) ≤ χ ^ p , 0 , (1-p)^{d}\leq F_{0}(v,x)\leq\widehat{\chi}_{p,0}, 
 
 
 where the lower bound is the isolated open-root event and the upper bound
uses  X 0 ≤ | K v | X_{0}\leq|K_{v}| . This case will only be used with
 p < p c s p<p_{c}^{\rm s} , when site sharpness
 [ 2 , Theorem 2 and Section 6]  makes
 χ ^ p , 0 < ∞ \widehat{\chi}_{p,0}<\infty . 
 
 
 For completeness, define the diagonally invariant transport 
 

 
 
 T k ​ ( a , b ) = 𝟏 ​ { 𝒫 a , b ∈ K a ∩ L − ( k + 1 ) ​ ( a ) } . T_{k}(a,b)=\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{P}_{a},\ b\in K_{a}\cap L_{-(k+1)}(a)\}. 
 
 
 The conditional tilted mass-transport identity ( 5.30 ) gives 
 

 
 
 p ​ ∫ 0 1 F − ( k + 1 ) ​ ( v , y ) ​ 𝑑 y = 𝔼 p ​ ∑ a 𝟏 ​ { a = peak ⁡ ( K v ) , a ∈ L k + 1 ​ ( v ) } ​ Δ ​ ( v , a ) . p\int_{0}^{1}F_{-(k+1)}(v,y)\,dy=\mathbb{E}_{p}\sum_{a}\mathbf{1}\{a=\operatorname{peak}(K_{v}),\ a\in L_{k+1}(v)\}\Delta(v,a). 
 
 (5.68) 
 
 The expectation on the right-hand side is over both the site
configuration and the independent uniform layer offset.
Indeed, the exact shift rule for the equivariant layers makes
 v ∈ L − ( k + 1 ) ​ ( a ) v\in L_{-(k+1)}(a)  equivalent to  a ∈ L k + 1 ​ ( v ) a\in L_{k+1}(v) .
For  a ∈ L k + 1 x ​ ( v ) a\in L_{k+1}^{x}(v) , 
 

 
 
 e t 0 ​ ( k + x ) < Δ ⁡ ( v , a ) ≤ e t 0 ​ ( k + 1 + x ) , e^{t_{0}(k+x)}<\Delta(v,a)\leq e^{t_{0}(k+1+x)}, 
 
 
 so this modular weight is comparable to  e t 0 ​ k e^{t_{0}k} , uniformly in the
offset. Together with ( 5.65 ), ( 5.68 ) gives 
 

 
 
 ∫ 0 1 F − ( k + 1 ) ( v , y ) d y ≍ p e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ k . \int_{0}^{1}F_{-(k+1)}(v,y)\,dy\asymp_{p}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)k}. 
 
 
 For  k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , two applications of ( 5.66 ) compare layers
 − ( k + 1 ) -(k+1)  and  − k -k , and ( 5.67 ) then replaces the offset average by
any fixed  x x . The case  k = 0 k=0  follows from the displayed bounds for
 F 0 F_{0} . Consequently 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x [ X − k − ∞ , ∞ ; 𝒫 v ] ≍ p e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ k . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[X_{-k}^{-\infty,\infty};\mathcal{P}_{v}]\asymp_{p}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)k}. 
 
 (5.69) 
 
 Every comparison constant above is independent of  v , x , k v,x,k , so the
constants in ( 5.69 ) are uniform in these variables.
For  p < p c s p<p_{c}^{\rm s} , put
 Y k = X − k − ∞ , ∞ ​ ( v ) ​ 𝟏 𝒫 v Y_{k}=X_{-k}^{-\infty,\infty}(v)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{P}_{v}} . Since
 Y k > 0 Y_{k}>0  precisely when  v ↔ L − k ​ ( v ) v\leftrightarrow L_{-k}(v)  and
 𝒫 v \mathcal{P}_{v}  occur, ( 5.69 ) and Cauchy–Schwarz give 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( Y k > 0 ) ≥ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ Y k ] 2 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ Y k 2 ] ≥ c p ​ e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ k , \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(Y_{k}>0)\geq\frac{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[Y_{k}]^{2}}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[Y_{k}^{2}]}\geq c_{p}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)k}, 
 
 (5.70) 
 
 where ( 5.61 ) with  ℓ = k \ell=k  bounds the denominator after the peak
indicator is deleted. Markov’s inequality and ( 5.69 ) give the reverse
probability bound. Moreover, on  { Y k > 0 } \{Y_{k}>0\}  one has
 X − k − ∞ , ∞ ≥ 1 X_{-k}^{-\infty,\infty}\geq 1 . Hence, for  ℓ ≥ 0 \ell\geq 0 , 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ X − ℓ − ∞ , ∞ ; Y k > 0 ] ≤ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ X − ℓ − ∞ , ∞ ​ X − k − ∞ , ∞ ] ≤ C p ​ e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ ( k ∨ ℓ ) . \begin{split}&\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[X_{-\ell}^{-\infty,\infty};Y_{k}>0]\\
&\qquad\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[X_{-\ell}^{-\infty,\infty}X_{-k}^{-\infty,\infty}]\leq C_{p}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)(k\vee\ell)}.\end{split} 
 
 (5.71) 
 
 Dividing ( 5.71 ) by the lower bound ( 5.70 ) proves the uniform
subcritical peak-survival estimates 
 
 
 
 
 ℙ ^ p v , x ( v ↔ L − k ( v ) , 𝒫 v ) \displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{-k}(v),\mathcal{P}_{v}) 
 ≍ p e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ k , \displaystyle\asymp_{p}e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)k}, 
 
 (5.72) 
 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x [ X − ℓ − ∞ , ∞ ∣ v ↔ L − k , 𝒫 v ] \displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[X_{-\ell}^{-\infty,\infty}\mid v\leftrightarrow L_{-k},\mathcal{P}_{v}] 
 ≤ C p ​ { 1 , ℓ ≤ k , e − t 0 ​ ( β p s − 1 ) ​ ( ℓ − k ) , ℓ > k . \displaystyle\leq C_{p}\begin{cases}1,&\ell\leq k,\\
e^{-t_{0}(\beta_{p}^{\rm s}-1)(\ell-k)},&\ell>k.\end{cases} 
 
 
 No bond coordinate occurs in this derivation. 
 
 
 We establish the lower critical exponent now, before using any critical
peak or fractional estimate. For  p < p c s p<p_{c}^{\rm s} , site sharpness
 [ 2 , Theorem 2 and Section 6]  gives
 χ ^ p , 0 < ∞ \widehat{\chi}_{p,0}<\infty . The tilted MTP gives
 χ ^ p , 1 = χ ^ p , 0 \widehat{\chi}_{p,1}=\widehat{\chi}_{p,0} , and log-convexity in the tilt
gives  χ ^ p , 1 / 2 < ∞ \widehat{\chi}_{p,1/2}<\infty . Hence  p < p t s p<p_{t}^{\rm s} , and
( 5.59 )–( 5.60 ) imply
 α p s = β p s > 1 \alpha_{p}^{\rm s}=\beta_{p}^{\rm s}>1 . Left continuity ( 5.3 ) now gives 
 

 
 
 α p c s s ≥ 1 . \alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}\geq 1. 
 
 (5.73) 
 
 
 
 Conditional Reimer and bounded surgery.  Fix a finite outcome
 ( A , D ) (A,D)  of the cluster kernel and let  J = V ∖ ( A ∪ D ) J=V\setminus(A\cup D) . For
cylinder events  E A , D E_{A,D}  and  B A , D B_{A,D}  in the coordinates of  J J ,
write  E A , D □ B A , D E_{A,D}\mathbin{\square}B_{A,D}  for disjoint occurrence. If
 E A , D ∩ B A , D ⊆ E A , D □ B A , D E_{A,D}\cap B_{A,D}\subseteq E_{A,D}\mathbin{\square}B_{A,D} , Lemma  2.2 
on the conditional product space ( 5.36 ) gives 
 

 
 
 ℙ p ​ ( E A , D ∩ B A , D ∣ A , D , R v = x ) ≤ ℙ p ​ ( E A , D ∣ A , D , R v = x ) ​ ℙ p ​ ( B A , D ∣ A , D , R v = x ) . \mathbb{P}_{p}(E_{A,D}\cap B_{A,D}\mid A,D,R_{v}=x)\leq\mathbb{P}_{p}(E_{A,D}\mid A,D,R_{v}=x)\mathbb{P}_{p}(B_{A,D}\mid A,D,R_{v}=x). 
 
 (5.74) 
 
 The infinite events used below will be exhausted by explicit cylinder
events; no continuity property of the operation  □ \square  will be needed. 
 
 
 We shall also use the following finite-coordinate map on that conditional
product space. Let  γ = ( q 0 , … , q s ) \gamma=(q_{0},\ldots,q_{s})  be a deterministic path with
 q 0 ∈ A q_{0}\in A  and  q 1 , … , q s ∉ A ∪ D q_{1},\ldots,q_{s}\notin A\cup D . On an event  E E , let
 C C  be the open cluster of  q s q_{s}  in a specified deterministic domain
after deleting  A ∪ D A\cup D . Define  Φ γ \Phi_{\gamma}  by opening
 q 1 , … , q s q_{1},\ldots,q_{s}  and closing every site adjacent to
 γ ∖ { q 0 } \gamma\setminus\{q_{0}\}  that is outside
 A ∪ D ∪ C ∪ γ A\cup D\cup C\cup\gamma . It leaves every other coordinate unchanged.
The image off- A A  cluster of  q s q_{s}  is contained in
 C ∪ ( γ ∖ A ) C\cup(\gamma\setminus A) , so a nonconnection event for  C C  is
preserved whenever  γ ∖ A \gamma\setminus A  avoids its forbidden set. The
map changes only coordinates in 
 

 
 
 S γ = { z ∈ J : d ⁡ ( z , γ ∖ { q 0 } ) ≤ 1 } , | S γ | ≤ M := ( d + 1 ) ​ s . S_{\gamma}=\{z\in J:d(z,\gamma\setminus\{q_{0}\})\leq 1\},\qquad|S_{\gamma}|\leq M:=(d+1)s. 
 
 
 Condition on all coordinates in  J ∖ S γ J\setminus S_{\gamma} . For every fixed
outside configuration,  Φ γ \Phi_{\gamma}  is a map of the finite space
 { 0 , 1 } S γ \{0,1\}^{S_{\gamma}} , every image has at most  2 M 2^{M}  preimages, and the
ratio of the Bernoulli weights of any two configurations in this space is
at most  [ p ⁡ ( 1 − p ) ] − M [p(1-p)]^{-M} . Summing over the finite fibres and then
integrating the outside configuration gives directly on the countable
product space 
 

 
 
 ℙ p ​ ( E ∣ A , D , R v = x ) ≤ 2 M ​ [ p ⁡ ( 1 − p ) ] − M ​ ℙ p ​ ( Φ γ ​ ( E ) ∣ A , D , R v = x ) . \mathbb{P}_{p}(E\mid A,D,R_{v}=x)\leq 2^{M}[p(1-p)]^{-M}\mathbb{P}_{p}(\Phi_{\gamma}(E)\mid A,D,R_{v}=x). 
 
 (5.75) 
 
 Thus ( 5.75 ) requires no finite-volume limit, even when  E E  contains
a nonconnection event: membership of a site in  C C  is measurable as a
countable union of finite path events, while the map changes only the fixed
finite set  S γ S_{\gamma} . In our application the two non-root connector
sites lie strictly below the induced graph used for the exploration, so
every coordinate actually changed by  Φ γ \Phi_{\gamma}  is untested. Sites
in  D D  are excluded from the map and left unchanged. No tested closed
site is reopened. 
 
 
 We record the resulting comparison as an actual site statement. Let
 𝒫 v ​ ( s ) \mathcal{P}_{v}(s)  be the event that  v v  is the peak of the set joined
to it inside  L − s , ∞ ​ ( v ) L_{-s,\infty}(v) , and let 
 

 
 
 𝒬 v ; k , ℓ , r = { v ↮ L ℓ − 1 ​ ( v ) ​  in  ​ L − k − r − 2 , ∞ ​ ( v ) } . \mathcal{Q}_{v;k,\ell,r}=\{v\not\leftrightarrow L_{\ell-1}(v)\text{ in }L_{-k-r-2,\infty}(v)\}. 
 
 
 There is  C p < ∞ C_{p}<\infty , depending only on  G , p G,p , such that for every
 0 < p ≤ p c s 0<p\leq p_{c}^{\rm s} ,  k , r ≥ 0 k,r\geq 0 , and  ℓ ≥ 2 \ell\geq 2 , 
 

 
 
 sup v , x 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ X − k − k − r , 0 ​ ( v ) ; 𝒬 v ; k , ℓ , r ] ≤ C p ​ inf v , x ∑ i = 0 3 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ X − k − ℓ − i − k − ℓ − r − 3 , 0 ​ ( v ) ∣ 𝒫 v ​ ( k + ℓ + r + 3 ) ] ℙ ^ p v , x ( v ↔ L − ℓ ( v ) ∣ 𝒫 v ( k + ℓ + r + 3 ) ) . \begin{split}&\sup_{v,x}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[X_{-k}^{-k-r,0}(v);\mathcal{Q}_{v;k,\ell,r}]\\
&\quad\leq C_{p}\inf_{v,x}\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{3}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[X_{-k-\ell-i}^{-k-\ell-r-3,0}(v)\mid\mathcal{P}_{v}(k+\ell+r+3)]}{\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{-\ell}(v)\mid\mathcal{P}_{v}(k+\ell+r+3))}.\end{split} 
 
 (5.76) 
 
 The denominator is nonzero. Indeed, from the conditioned-open root,
force the  ℓ \ell -step maximal-decrement path until it reaches
 L − ℓ L_{-\ell} 
and close every exterior neighbor of that finite path inside
 L − k − ℓ − r − 3 , ∞ L_{-k-\ell-r-3,\infty} . This finite-coordinate event has positive
probability, the root reaches  L − ℓ L_{-\ell} , and the root is the peak of
the resulting restricted cluster. More precisely, the path has
 ℓ \ell  non-root sites and its external boundary in the displayed slab
has at most  d ⁡ ( ℓ + 1 ) d(\ell+1)  sites. After omitting any boundary site that
also lies on the path, the conditional product probability of the
prescription is 
 

 
 
 p ℓ ​ ( 1 − p ) N ∂ > 0 , N ∂ ≤ d ⁡ ( ℓ + 1 ) . p^{\ell}(1-p)^{N_{\partial}}>0,\qquad N_{\partial}\leq d(\ell+1). 
 
 
 Every non-root path vertex has strictly negative modular height relative
to  v v , and the closed external boundary prevents the restricted
cluster from containing any other site. Hence the prescription is
contained in the intersection of the numerator event and the
conditioning peak event. The peak event itself has positive probability
because it contains the isolated open-root event. Division by that
positive probability proves that the displayed conditional denominator
is strictly positive. 
 
 
 Here is the derivation. Fix  v , x v,x , set
 H + ​ ( v ) = { z : log ⁡ Δ ⁡ ( v , z ) ≥ 0 } ∖ { v } H^{+}(v)=\{z:\log\Delta(v,z)\geq 0\}\setminus\{v\} , and expose the
open cluster  A A  of  v v  in the induced graph on
 L − ℓ , ∞ ​ ( v ) L_{-\ell,\infty}(v) , together with its tested external boundary
 D D  in that induced graph. Retain only outcomes on which  v v  is the
peak of  A A  and  A ∩ L − ℓ ≠ ∅ A\cap L_{-\ell}\neq\varnothing , and select
 u u  in this intersection. At  p ≤ p c s p\leq p_{c}^{\rm s} ,  A A  and every
residual cluster used below are finite almost surely by the
Auxiliary Lemma  5.F . 
 
 
 Let  q 1 = u − q_{1}=u^{-}  and  q = q 1 − q=q_{1}^{-}  be two successive
maximal-decrement neighbors of  u u . The reverse of ( 5.7 ) and the exact
shift rule give 
 

 
 
 q 1 ∈ L − ℓ − 1 ​ ( v ) , q ∈ L − ℓ − 2 ​ ( v ) , L j ​ ( q ) = L j − ℓ − 2 ​ ( v ) . q_{1}\in L_{-\ell-1}(v),\quad q\in L_{-\ell-2}(v),\quad L_{j}(q)=L_{j-\ell-2}(v). 
 
 (5.77) 
 
 Since the cluster was explored in the induced graph
 L − ℓ , ∞ ​ ( v ) L_{-\ell,\infty}(v) , neither  q 1 q_{1}  nor  q q  was tested. Put 
 

 
 
 D ∗ = L − k − ℓ − r − 3 , ∞ ​ ( v ) ∖ ( A ∪ D ) D_{*}=L_{-k-\ell-r-3,\infty}(v)\setminus(A\cup D) 
 
 
 and, for  w ∈ L − k − 1 , − k + 1 ​ ( q ) w\in L_{-k-1,-k+1}(q) , let  E q , w A , D E_{q,w}^{A,D}  be the event
that  q q  is open, that it is joined to  w w  in
 L − k − ℓ − r − 3 , − ℓ − 1 ​ ( v ) ∖ ( A ∪ D ) L_{-k-\ell-r-3,-\ell-1}(v)\setminus(A\cup D) , and that its open cluster in
 D ∗ D_{*}  does not meet  H + ​ ( v ) H^{+}(v) . Let  B A , D B^{A,D}  be the event that an
open path leaves  A A  through an untested site below  L − ℓ L_{-\ell} ,
uses no site of  D D , and returns to  H + ​ ( v ) H^{+}(v)  inside  D ∗ D_{*} . Thus
 B A , D B^{A,D}  is exactly the competing off- A A  continuation that
destroys the restricted peak when the lower coordinates are restored.
We now justify the infinite-volume Reimer step. Put 
 

 
 
 S ∗ = L − k − ℓ − r − 3 , − ℓ − 1 ​ ( v ) ∖ ( A ∪ D ) S_{*}=L_{-k-\ell-r-3,-\ell-1}(v)\setminus(A\cup D) 
 
 
 and let  C q C_{q}  be the open cluster of  q q  in  D ∗ D_{*} , with

 Λ N = B N ​ ( v ) ∩ J \Lambda_{N}=B_{N}(v)\cap J , and, once  q , w ∈ Λ N q,w\in\Lambda_{N} , define 
 
 
 
 
 ℰ N = \displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{N}={} 
 { q  open , q ↔ w  in  S ∗ ∩ Λ N , C q ⊆ Λ N , C q ∩ H + ( v ) = ∅ } , \displaystyle\{q\text{ open},\ q\leftrightarrow w\text{ in }S_{*}\cap\Lambda_{N},\ C_{q}\subseteq\Lambda_{N},\ C_{q}\cap H^{+}(v)=\varnothing\}, 
 
 (5.78) 
 
 
 ℬ N = \displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{N}={} 
 { a path defining  B A , D  exists with every
vertex outside  A  in  Λ N } . \displaystyle\{\text{a path defining }B^{A,D}\text{ exists with every
vertex outside }A\text{ in }\Lambda_{N}\}. 
 
 
 To match Auxiliary Lemma  5.E  literally,
let  T A , D ⊆ D ∗ T_{A,D}\subseteq D_{*}  be the residual sites below
 L − ℓ ​ ( v ) L_{-\ell}(v)  that are adjacent to  A A . Any path defining

 F ∗ = H + ​ ( v ) ∩ D ∗ F_{*}=H^{+}(v)\cap D_{*} . Conversely, an open  T A , D T_{A,D} -to- F ∗ F_{*} 
path in  D ∗ D_{*} , preceded by an open path in  A A  to its initial

coordinates are omitted,  ℬ N \mathcal{B}_{N}  is precisely the event 
 

 
 
 T A , D ↔ F ∗ in  ​ D ∗ ∩ Λ N , T_{A,D}\leftrightarrow F_{*}\quad\hbox{in }D_{*}\cap\Lambda_{N}, 
 
 
 whereas  ℰ N \mathcal{E}_{N}  is the lemma’s event with
 D 0 = D ∗ D_{0}=D_{*} ,  S = S ∗ S=S_{*} ,  T = T A , D T=T_{A,D} ,  F = F ∗ F=F_{*} ,  u = q u=q , and the
present  w w . Thus the terminal sets, permitted domain, and forbidden

Both are cylinder events. For  ℬ N \mathcal{B}_{N}  this follows from the
finiteness of  Λ N \Lambda_{N} . For  ℰ N \mathcal{E}_{N} , explore the cluster of
 q q  in  D ∗ ∩ Λ N D_{*}\cap\Lambda_{N} ; the condition
 C q ⊆ Λ N C_{q}\subseteq\Lambda_{N}  is certified by the states in this finite set
and its finite  D ∗ D_{*} -external boundary. 
 
 
 On  ℰ N \mathcal{E}_{N} , let 
 

 
 
 ∂ D ∗ C q = { z ∈ D ∗ ∖ C q : z ∼ y ​  for some  ​ y ∈ C q } . \partial_{D_{*}}C_{q}=\{z\in D_{*}\setminus C_{q}:z\sim y\text{ for some }y\in C_{q}\}. 
 
 
 The finite set  C q ∪ ∂ D ∗ C q C_{q}\cup\partial_{D_{*}}C_{q} , with the sites of  C q C_{q} 
prescribed open and those of  ∂ D ∗ C q \partial_{D_{*}}C_{q}  prescribed closed, is

 S ∗ ∩ Λ N S_{*}\cap\Lambda_{N} . A selected open path

segment to  H + ​ ( v ) H^{+}(v)  would join  q q  to  H + ​ ( v ) H^{+}(v)  in  D ∗ D_{*} . It cannot meet
 ∂ D ∗ C q \partial_{D_{*}}C_{q} , since every site in that boundary is closed.
After omitting the already conditioned coordinates in  A ∪ D A\cup D , these
are disjoint witness sets. Hence 
 

 
 
 ℰ N ∩ ℬ N ⊆ ℰ N □ ℬ N . \mathcal{E}_{N}\cap\mathcal{B}_{N}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{N}\mathbin{\square}\mathcal{B}_{N}. 
 
 
 
 
 For  p < p c s p<p_{c}^{\rm s} , every open cluster is finite almost surely by the
definition of  p c s p_{c}^{\rm s} ; at  p = p c s p=p_{c}^{\rm s} , the same holds by the
Auxiliary Lemma  5.F . A finite-coordinate
conditioning of positive
probability preserves this almost-sure statement, and  C q C_{q}  is a
subcluster of the full cluster. Consequently, conditional on every
admissible  ( A , D ) (A,D)  and  R v = x R_{v}=x , 
 

 
 
 ℰ N ↑ E q , w A , D , ℬ N ↑ B A , D modulo a null set . \mathcal{E}_{N}\uparrow E_{q,w}^{A,D},\qquad\mathcal{B}_{N}\uparrow B^{A,D}\quad\text{modulo a null set}. 
 
 
 Apply ( 5.74 ) to  ℰ N , ℬ N \mathcal{E}_{N},\mathcal{B}_{N}  and use continuity from
below separately for these two nested sequences and for their
intersections. This gives 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( E q , w A , D ∩ B A , D ∣ A , D ) ≤ ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( E q , w A , D ∣ A , D ) ​ ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( B A , D ∣ A , D ) . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(E_{q,w}^{A,D}\cap B^{A,D}\mid A,D)\leq\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(E_{q,w}^{A,D}\mid A,D)\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(B^{A,D}\mid A,D). 
 
 (5.79) 
 
 Thus ( 5.79 ) uses only finite Reimer inequalities and ordinary
continuity from below, not continuity of disjoint occurrence and not an
independence assertion. 
 
 
 Write  R = E q , w A , D R=E_{q,w}^{A,D} ,  B = B A , D B=B^{A,D} , and take every probability in
the conditional product space left after  ( A , D ) (A,D)  has been exposed. If
 0 < ℙ ⁡ ( B ) < 1 0<\mathbb{P}(B)<1 , ( 5.79 ) gives 
 

 
 
 ℙ ⁡ ( R ∣ B c ) − ℙ ⁡ ( R ) = ℙ ⁡ ( B ) 1 − ℙ ⁡ ( B ) ​ [ ℙ ⁡ ( R ) − ℙ ⁡ ( R ∣ B ) ] ≥ 0 . \mathbb{P}(R\mid B^{c})-\mathbb{P}(R)=\frac{\mathbb{P}(B)}{1-\mathbb{P}(B)}[\mathbb{P}(R)-\mathbb{P}(R\mid B)]\geq 0. 
 
 (5.80) 
 
 The same conclusion is immediate when  ℙ ⁡ ( B ) = 0 \mathbb{P}(B)=0 . If
 ℙ ⁡ ( B ) = 1 \mathbb{P}(B)=1 , the cluster outcome has zero probability together
with the restricted-peak event and contributes nothing to the later
conditional average. On the exact-cluster outcome,  B c B^{c}  is precisely
the assertion that  v v  remains the peak when the lower coordinates are
restored. Apply the map ( 5.75 ) with  γ = ( u , q 1 , q ) \gamma=(u,q_{1},q)  to
 R ∩ B c R\cap B^{c} . It preserves both  B c B^{c}  and the off- A A  nonconnection in
 E q , w A , D E_{q,w}^{A,D} : opening the connector only attaches  A A  to the
off- A A  cluster  C C , which avoids  H + ​ ( v ) H^{+}(v) , while every other new
exit from the connector is closed by the map. Its cost is a constant
depending only on  d , p d,p .
On the image of  R ∩ B c R\cap B^{c} , the open path in  A A , the connector
 γ \gamma , and the path from  q q  to  w w  concatenate. By ( 5.77 ), a
site in  L − k + j ​ ( q ) L_{-k+j}(q) ,  − 1 ≤ j ≤ 1 -1\leq j\leq 1 , belongs respectively to
 L − k − ℓ − i ​ ( v ) L_{-k-\ell-i}(v)  with  i = 3 , 2 , 1 i=3,2,1 . All these concatenated paths lie
in  L − k − ℓ − r − 3 , 0 ​ ( v ) L_{-k-\ell-r-3,0}(v) . Summing ( 5.80 ) over  w w , and then using
the finite-map bound ( 5.75 ), therefore gives, for every admissible
 ( A , D ) (A,D) , the following. Write
 𝔼 ^ p , J q , R q ; A , D \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p,J}^{q,R_{q};A,D}  for expectation under the
residual product law on  J J , with  A , D A,D  held fixed and  q q  conditioned
to be open. Then 
 

 
 
 ∑ i = 0 3 𝔼 ^ p v , x [ X − k − ℓ − i − k − ℓ − r − 3 , 0 ( v ) ∣ A , D , 𝒫 v ( k + ℓ + r + 3 ) ] ≥ c d , p ​ 𝔼 ^ p , J q , R q ; A , D ​ [ Z q ; ℰ q ] , \begin{split}&\sum_{i=0}^{3}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[X_{-k-\ell-i}^{-k-\ell-r-3,0}(v)\mid A,D,\mathcal{P}_{v}(k+\ell+r+3)]\\
&\qquad\geq c_{d,p}\,\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p,J}^{q,R_{q};A,D}[Z_{q};\mathcal{E}_{q}],\end{split}

 The constant  c d , p > 0 c_{d,p}>0  is the reciprocal of the constant in
( 5.75 ) (including the probability that the open-root site  q q  is
open before the map is applied). Thus
( 5.81 ) is a statement on the conditional product space ( 5.36 ); its
domain, changed coordinates, event preservation, and multiplicity have
all been specified. 
 
 
 It remains only to compare offsets, and this step is deterministic. Fix
an arbitrary  y ∈ [ 0 , 1 ) y\in[0,1) , use the same site configuration, and write
 L j y ​ ( q ) L_{j}^{y}(q)  and  X j m , n ​ ( q , y ) X_{j}^{m,n}(q;y)  for the layers and counts obtained by
setting  R q = y R_{q}=y . Directly from
the half-open definition, 
 

 
 
 L − k y ​ ( q ) ⊆ L − k − 1 , − k + 1 R q ​ ( q ) , L − k − r , 0 y ​ ( q ) ⊆ L − k − r − 1 , 1 R q ​ ( q ) , L − k − r − 1 , ∞ R q ​ ( q ) ⊆ L − k − r − 2 , ∞ y ​ ( q ) . \begin{split}L_{-k}^{y}(q)&\subseteq L_{-k-1,-k+1}^{R_{q}}(q),\\

 By ( 5.77 ), the left side of the last inclusion is the layer half-space
underlying  D ∗ D_{*}  before deletion of  A ∪ D A\cup D . Moreover, every path from  q q  to
 H + ​ ( v ) H^{+}(v)  meets  L ℓ − 1 y ​ ( q ) L_{\ell-1}^{y}(q) : its terminal modular height
relative to  q q  is greater than  t 0 ​ ℓ t_{0}\ell , while an edge changes
that height by at most  t 0 t_{0} .
Consequently 
 

 
 
 { 𝒬 q ; k , ℓ , r ​  for offset  ​ y } ⊆ ℰ q , \{\mathcal{Q}_{q;k,\ell,r}\text{ for offset }y\}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{q}, 
 
 
 because deleting  A ∪ D A\cup D  can only remove possible paths. The first

 (5.83) 
 
 We now remove the residual conditioning without appealing to

is  L 1 ​ ( q ) = L − ℓ − 1 ​ ( v ) L_{1}(q)=L_{-\ell-1}(v) , strictly below the explored region
 L − ℓ , ∞ ​ ( v ) L_{-\ell,\infty}(v) . Hence every coordinate inspected by  Z q Z_{q}  lies

 D ∗ ⊆ J D_{*}\subseteq J , so
 Z q ​ 𝟏 ℰ q Z_{q}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{q}}  is also measurable with respect to the
residual coordinates alone. 
 
 
 Extend the residual product measure conditioned on  q q  being open by
assigning fresh, independent Bernoulli variables with parameter  p p  to
every site of  A ∪ D A\cup D . This extension is the ordinary product
measure conditioned only on  q q  being open; the fresh variables are
