( 5.83 ) uses only coordinates in  J J , extending the measure does not
change its expectation. On every extended configuration, the pointwise
inclusion  𝒬 q ; k , ℓ , r y ⊆ ℰ q \mathcal{Q}_{q;k,\ell,r}^{\,y}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{q}  and the
two count inclusions in ( 5.82 ) still give ( 5.83 ). Consequently, for
every  y ∈ [ 0 , 1 ) y\in[0,1) , 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p , J q , R q ; A , D ​ [ Z q ; ℰ q ] ≥ 𝔼 ^ p q , y ​ [ X − k − k − r , 0 ​ ( q ) ; 𝒬 q ; k , ℓ , r ] . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p,J}^{q,R_{q};A,D}[Z_{q};\mathcal{E}_{q}]\geq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{q,y}[X_{-k}^{-k-r,0}(q);\mathcal{Q}_{q;k,\ell,r}]. 
 
 
 Only now do we use transitivity: the supremum over  y y  of the last
ordinary open-root expectation is independent of  q q , and hence equals
the supremum over both roots and offsets on the left side of ( 5.76 ).
Thus ( 5.81 ) is bounded below, uniformly in the exposed pair  ( A , D ) (A,D) ,
by  c d , p c_{d,p}  times that supremum. Finally average ( 5.81 ) over precisely
the cluster outcomes reaching  L − ℓ L_{-\ell}  under
 𝒫 v ​ ( k + ℓ + r + 3 ) \mathcal{P}_{v}(k+\ell+r+3) , and divide by their conditional
probability. Since this holds for every  v , x v,x , take the infimum on the
right. This proves ( 5.76 ) with  C p = c d , p − 1 C_{p}=c_{d,p}^{-1} . Only values
 ℓ ≥ 2 \ell\geq 2  are used below; enlarging the auxiliary  ℓ \ell  to two
absorbs the finitely many smaller cases in estimates not involving
( 5.76 ). 
 
 
 Critical peak survival.  We give the parameter-uniform argument in
full. First, 
 

 
 
 q R := sup v ∈ V , x ∈ [ 0 , 1 ) ℙ ^ p c s v , x ​ ( v ↔ L R , ∞ ​ ( v ) ) ⟶ 0 . q_{R}:=\sup_{v\in V,\,x\in[0,1)}\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{R,\infty}(v))\longrightarrow 0. 
 
 (5.84) 
 
 Indeed, after enlarging the target by one layer, the event is contained
in the raw-height event that the open cluster reaches height
 t 0 ​ ( R − 1 ) t_{0}(R-1) . These events decrease to the event that the cluster has
unbounded height, which has probability zero by the critical-cluster
lemma. Conditioning the root open costs only  ( p c s ) − 1 (p_{c}^{\rm s})^{-1} , and
transitivity plus the one-layer enlargement makes the bound uniform in
the root and offset. 
 
 
 Fix  ζ > 0 \zeta>0 . By left continuity choose
 p − = p c s − δ p_{-}=p_{c}^{\rm s}-\delta  so close to  p c s p_{c}^{\rm s}  that 
 

 
 
 α p − s ≤ α p c s s + ζ / 4 . \alpha_{p_{-}}^{\rm s}\leq\alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}+\zeta/4. 
 
 (5.85) 
 
 Couple the two parameters by opening each  p − p_{-} -closed site, independently,
with probability
 ρ = ( p c s − p − ) / ( 1 − p − ) \rho=(p_{c}^{\rm s}-p_{-})/(1-p_{-}) . Under the conditional-open-root
coupling, the root label is sampled in  [ 0 , p − ] [0,p_{-}] , so it is open at both
levels and is not resampled. By ( 5.72 ), uniformly in  v , x , k , ℓ v,x,k,\ell , 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p − v , x ( v ↔ L − k ( v ) , 𝒫 v ) ≥ c − e − t 0 ​ ( α p − s − 1 ) ​ k , 𝔼 ^ p − v , x [ X − ℓ − ∞ , ∞ ∣ v ↔ L − k , 𝒫 v ] ≤ C − { 1 , ℓ ≤ k , e − t 0 ​ ( α p − s − 1 ) ​ ( ℓ − k ) , ℓ > k . \begin{split}&\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p_{-}}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{-k}(v),\mathcal{P}_{v})\geq c_{-}e^{-t_{0}(\alpha_{p_{-}}^{\rm s}-1)k},\\
&\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p_{-}}^{v,x}[X_{-\ell}^{-\infty,\infty}\mid v\leftrightarrow L_{-k},\mathcal{P}_{v}]\leq C_{-}\begin{cases}1,&\ell\leq k,\\
e^{-t_{0}(\alpha_{p_{-}}^{\rm s}-1)(\ell-k)},&\ell>k.\end{cases}\end{split} 
 
 (5.86) 
 
 
 
 Write 
 

 
 
 𝒜 k − = { v ↔ L − k ( v ) , 𝒫 v } in the  p − -configuration . \mathcal{A}_{k}^{-}=\{v\leftrightarrow L_{-k}(v),\ \mathcal{P}_{v}\}\quad\text{in the $p_{-}$-configuration}. 
 
 
 Condition on a finite  p − p_{-} -cluster  A A  for which  𝒜 k − \mathcal{A}_{k}^{-} 
occurs. Assign each site of  ∂ V A \partial_{V}A  to its first
incident vertex in  A A ; at most  d d  boundary sites are assigned to
one vertex. For vertices in layers  L − R + 1 , 0 ​ ( v ) L_{-R+1,0}(v) , require all assigned

has conditional probability at least 
 

 
 
 ( 1 − ρ ) d ​ | A ∩ L − R + 1 , 0 | . (1-\rho)^{d|A\cap L_{-R+1,0}|}. 
 
 (5.87) 
 
 For a vertex in  L − ℓ ​ ( v ) L_{-\ell}(v) ,  ℓ ≥ R \ell\geq R , require instead that
none of its assigned boundary sites both opens by level  p c s p_{c}^{\rm s} 
and has an off- A A  open continuation to the raw upper half-space.
To make this conditional event explicit, for a boundary site  y y 
assigned to such a vertex define 
 

 
 
 D y = { U y > p c s } ∪ { p − < U y ≤ p c s , y  has no off- A  critical continuation to the upper half-space } . D_{y}=\{U_{y}>p_{c}^{\rm s}\}\cup\{p_{-}<U_{y}\leq p_{c}^{\rm s},\ y\text{ has no off-}A\text{ critical continuation to the upper half-space}\}. 
 
 
 Conditional on  A A , the boundary label  U y U_{y}  is uniform on
 ( p − , 1 ] (p_{-},1] , independently of every other residual label. Conditional
further on  p − < U y ≤ p c s p_{-}<U_{y}\leq p_{c}^{\rm s} , the first-hit residual kernel
couples the off- A A  cluster of  y y  below an independent critical
open-root cluster. Up to three layers of displacement (one for the
incident edge and two for the half-open layer endpoints),
( 5.84 ) therefore gives 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ ​ ( D y c ∣ A ) ≤ ρ ​ q ℓ − 3 ≤ q R − 3 . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}(D_{y}^{c}\mid A)\leq\rho q_{\ell-3}\leq q_{R-3}. 
 
 
 We take  R ≥ 4 R\geq 4 . 
 
 
 The indicators of the near-top requirements
 { U y > p c s } \{U_{y}>p_{c}^{\rm s}\}  and the far requirements  D y D_{y}  are all
coordinatewise increasing functions of the residual uniform labels;
equivalently, these events are decreasing in the induced open-site
configuration. The conditional law is a product law: boundary labels
are independent uniforms on  ( p − , 1 ] (p_{-},1] , and all other residual labels
are independent uniforms on  [ 0 , 1 ] [0,1] . Harris–FKG therefore bounds the
probability of their intersection below by the product of their
individual probabilities, even when different  D y D_{y} ’s inspect common
exterior coordinates. At most  d d  boundary sites are assigned to each
vertex of  A A . Since  1 − ρ ≤ 1 1-\rho\leq 1  and  1 − q R − 3 ≤ 1 1-q_{R-3}\leq 1 , replacing
the actual numbers of assigned sites by these upper bounds can only
decrease the product. We obtain 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ ​ ( 𝒫 v ​  survives at  ​ p c s ∣ A ) ≥ ( 1 − ρ ) d ​ | A ∩ L − R + 1 , 0 | ​ ( 1 − q R − 3 ) d ​ | A ∩ L − ∞ , − R | . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{P}_{v}\text{ survives at }p_{c}^{\rm s}\mid A)\geq(1-\rho)^{d|A\cap L_{-R+1,0}|}(1-q_{R-3})^{d|A\cap L_{-\infty,-R}|}. 
 
 (5.88) 
 
 If the requirements hold, take any critical path from  A A  to a vertex
of nonnegative raw height. The subcritical peak event ensures that the
path is not contained in  A A . Let  u u  be its last vertex in  A A 
and  y y  the following vertex. Then  y ∉ A y\notin A ,  y y  is an assigned
boundary site, and the entire suffix beginning at  y y  is disjoint from
 A A . If  u u  is in one of the top  R R  layers,
( 5.87 ) says that  y y  is closed. Otherwise this suffix is
exactly the off- A A  continuation forbidden in the far-layer
requirement. Both alternatives are contradictions, so  v v  remains
the peak. This also handles a boundary site adjacent to several
vertices: it is assigned only once, and whichever requirement is imposed
either closes that boundary site or forbids the displayed off- A A 
continuation. 
 
 
 Choose  R = R ⁡ ( ζ , p − ) R=R(\zeta,p_{-})  so large that
 − d ​ log ⁡ ( 1 − q R − 3 ) ≤ t 0 ​ ζ / ( 8 ​ C − ) -d\log(1-q_{R-3})\leq t_{0}\zeta/(8C_{-}) . Conditional on the event in
( 5.86 ), put 
 

 
 
 N top = | A ∩ L − R + 1 , 0 | , N deep = | A ∩ L − ∞ , − R | . N_{\rm top}=|A\cap L_{-R+1,0}|,\qquad N_{\rm deep}=|A\cap L_{-\infty,-R}|. 
 
 
 The second line of ( 5.86 ), summed over layers, gives 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p − v , x ​ [ N top ∣ 𝒜 k − ] ≤ C − ​ R , 𝔼 ^ p − v , x ​ [ N deep ∣ 𝒜 k − ] ≤ C − ​ k + C − ​ ∑ s ≥ 1 e − t 0 ​ ( α p − s − 1 ) ​ s . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p_{-}}^{v,x}[N_{\rm top}\mid\mathcal{A}_{k}^{-}]\leq C_{-}R,\qquad\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p_{-}}^{v,x}[N_{\rm deep}\mid\mathcal{A}_{k}^{-}]\leq C_{-}k+C_{-}\sum_{s\geq 1}e^{-t_{0}(\alpha_{p_{-}}^{\rm s}-1)s}. 
 
 (5.89) 
 
 The series is finite because  p − < p c s p_{-}<p_{c}^{\rm s}  and hence
 α p − s > 1 \alpha_{p_{-}}^{\rm s}>1 . Write the right side of ( 5.88 ) as
 exp ⁡ [ − a ​ N top − b ​ N deep ] \exp[-aN_{\rm top}-bN_{\rm deep}] , where
 a = − d ​ log ⁡ ( 1 − ρ ) a=-d\log(1-\rho)  and  b = − d ​ log ⁡ ( 1 − q R − 3 ) b=-d\log(1-q_{R-3}) . Since
 y ↦ e − y y\mapsto e^{-y}  is convex, conditional Jensen and ( 5.89 ) give a
constant factor depending on  R , p − R,p_{-}  times
 e − b ​ C − ​ k e^{-bC_{-}k} . The choice of  R R  makes this at least a constant times
 e − t 0 ζ k / 8 e^{-t_{0}\zeta k/8} , which is stronger than the following convenient
bound: 
 

 
 
 inf v , x ℙ ^ ( 𝒫 v  survives at  p c s ∣ v ↔ L − k , 𝒫 v  at  p − ) ≥ c ζ e − t 0 ζ k / 4 . \inf_{v,x}\widehat{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{P}_{v}\text{ survives at }p_{c}^{\rm s}\mid v\leftrightarrow L_{-k},\mathcal{P}_{v}\text{ at }p_{-})\geq c_{\zeta}e^{-t_{0}\zeta k/4}. 
 
 (5.90) 
 
 Under the monotone two-level coupling, the intersection of the
subcritical event in the first line of ( 5.86 ) and the survival event in
( 5.90 ) is contained in the corresponding critical peak event. Thus the
two lower bounds multiply. Using ( 5.85 ), their product is at least 
 

 
 
 c ζ ​ exp ⁡ { − t 0 ​ [ α p c s s − 1 + ζ / 2 ] ​ k } , c_{\zeta}\exp\{-t_{0}[\alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}-1+\zeta/2]k\}, 
 
 
 after absorbing the fixed layer shifts into  c ζ c_{\zeta} . Since this is
stronger than the same bound with  ζ / 2 \zeta/2  replaced by  ζ \zeta , it
proves the quantified critical estimate 
 

 
 
 inf v , x ℙ ^ p c s v , x ( v ↔ L − k ( v ) , 𝒫 v ) ≥ c ζ e − t 0 ​ ( α p c s s − 1 + ζ ) ​ k . \inf_{v,x}\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{v,x}(v\leftrightarrow L_{-k}(v),\mathcal{P}_{v})\geq c_{\zeta}e^{-t_{0}(\alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}-1+\zeta)k}. 
 
 (5.91) 
 
 All constants in ( 5.91 ) are chosen after  ζ \zeta  and before
 v , x , k v,x,k ; this is the uniform critical peak-survival estimate needed in
( 5.76 ). 
 
 
 Proposition 5.3   (Fractional layer recursions) . 
 
 In the canonical nonunimodular setup above, let
 0 < p ≤ p c s 0<p\leq p_{c}^{\rm s} . For  0 ≤ δ ≤ 1 0\leq\delta\leq 1  and every slab
containing layers  0 0  and  k k , the conditional-root tilted
mass-transport inequalities ( 5.93 ) hold. For
 0 ≤ ε , δ ≤ 1 0\leq\varepsilon,\delta\leq 1  and
 − ∞ ≤ m ≤ k ≤ n ≤ ∞ -\infty\leq m\leq k\leq n\leq\infty , the two up/down recursions
( 5.105 ) hold. If in addition
 0 ≤ δ ≤ ε < 1 0\leq\delta\leq\varepsilon<1  and the slab contains  0 0  and  k k ,
then the nonlinear Hölder estimate
( 5.106 ) holds. All expectations may be
extended nonnegative expectations, and every fixed-offset supremum is
taken only after the offset-averaged mass transport. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The conditioning event
 𝒫 v ​ ( s ) \mathcal{P}_{v}(s)  has probability at least  ( 1 − p ) d (1-p)^{d}  under the
open-root law, since it contains the event that every neighbor of the root
is closed. The additional survival probability in the denominator of
( 5.76 ) is not treated as constant; it is bounded by ( 5.91 ). 
 
 
 We first prove the Holder–MTP input. Throughout
( 5.92 )–( 5.106 ), let
 0 < p ≤ p c s 0<p\leq p_{c}^{\rm s} . Every open cluster is then finite almost surely.
For  p < p c s p<p_{c}^{\rm s} , the definition gives zero probability that any
fixed vertex lies in an infinite cluster, and countability of  V V  permits
a union over all vertices; at  p = p c s p=p_{c}^{\rm s} , this is the
Auxiliary Lemma  5.F . All subsequent uses take
 p = p c s p=p_{c}^{\rm s} . The
root is fixed and the independent offset
 R v R_{v}  is averaged; we suppress  v v  from the expectation and count notation.
Fix  0 ≤ δ ≤ 1 0\leq\delta\leq 1  and
 − ∞ ≤ m ≤ min ⁡ { 0 , k } ≤ max ⁡ { 0 , k } ≤ n ≤ ∞ -\infty\leq m\leq\min\{0,k\}\leq\max\{0,k\}\leq n\leq\infty ;
in particular the slab contains both the root layer and the target
layer. Apply ( 5.30 ) to the transport  F ⁡ ( u , z ) F(u,z)  defined by 
 

 
 
 ( X − k m − k , n − k ( u ) ) − δ 𝟏 { z ∈ L − k ( u ) , u ↔ z  in  L m − k , n − k ( u ) } , (X_{-k}^{m-k,n-k}(u))^{-\delta}\mathbf{1}\{z\in L_{-k}(u),\ u\leftrightarrow z\text{ in }L_{m-k,n-k}(u)\}, 
 
 (5.92) 
 
 where the mass is defined as zero when the indicator vanishes. The
negative power is therefore evaluated only when the count is at least
one, and both transported endpoints are open. The outgoing sum at
 u u  is exactly 
 

 
 
 ∑ z F ⁡ ( u , z ) = ( X − k m − k , n − k ​ ( u ) ) 1 − δ . \sum_{z}F(u,z)=(X_{-k}^{m-k,n-k}(u))^{1-\delta}. 
 
 
 We spell out the incoming sum. If  v ∈ L − k ​ ( u ) v\in L_{-k}(u) , equivariance of
the random offset in ( 5.6 ) gives 
 

 
 
 u ∈ L k ( v ) , L j ( u ) = L j + k ( v ) ( j ∈ ℤ ) . u\in L_{k}(v),\qquad L_{j}(u)=L_{j+k}(v)\quad(j\in\mathbb{Z}). 
 
 
 Consequently 
 

 
 
 L m − k , n − k ​ ( u ) = L m , n ​ ( v ) , X − k m − k , n − k ​ ( u ) = X 0 m , n ​ ( v ) . L_{m-k,n-k}(u)=L_{m,n}(v),\qquad X_{-k}^{m-k,n-k}(u)=X_{0}^{m,n}(v). 
 
 
 Moreover,  u ∈ L k ​ ( v ) u\in L_{k}(v)  implies 
 

 
 
 e t 0 ​ ( k − 1 ) < Δ ⁡ ( v , u ) ≤ e t 0 ​ ( k + 1 ) . e^{t_{0}(k-1)}<\Delta(v,u)\leq e^{t_{0}(k+1)}. 
 
 
 Thus the incoming sum at  v v , including the modular weight in
( 5.30 ), lies between 
 

 
 
 e t 0 ​ ( k − 1 ) ​ X k m , n ​ ( v ) ​ ( X 0 m , n ​ ( v ) ) − δ and e t 0 ​ ( k + 1 ) ​ X k m , n ​ ( v ) ​ ( X 0 m , n ​ ( v ) ) − δ . e^{t_{0}(k-1)}X_{k}^{m,n}(v)(X_{0}^{m,n}(v))^{-\delta}\quad\hbox{and}\quad e^{t_{0}(k+1)}X_{k}^{m,n}(v)(X_{0}^{m,n}(v))^{-\delta}. 
 
 
 Here both expressions are declared zero when  X k m , n ​ ( v ) = 0 X_{k}^{m,n}(v)=0 ; when
that count is positive,  X 0 m , n ​ ( v ) ≥ 1 X_{0}^{m,n}(v)\geq 1 . The cluster-finiteness
statement above makes every count appearing in the coefficient of
( 5.92 ) finite almost surely. Consequently the outgoing sum is
indeed  ( X − k m − k , n − k ​ ( u ) ) 1 − δ (X_{-k}^{m-k,n-k}(u))^{1-\delta} , including at  δ = 1 \delta=1 
under the convention  X 0 = 𝟏 { X > 0 } X^{0}=\mathbf{1}_{\{X>0\}} . To justify possibly
infinite expectations, multiply the transport by
 𝟏 { d ( u , z ) ≤ N } \mathbf{1}\{d(u,z)\leq N\} . Local finiteness makes both truncated sums
finite, so ( 5.30 ) applies. Since the untruncated coefficient is
now finite, letting  N ↑ ∞ N\uparrow\infty  and using monotone convergence
gives the asserted outgoing and incoming sums with extended nonnegative
expectations. Averaging the independent offset and using the two
pointwise modular bounds gives the exact inequalities 
 

 
 
 e t 0 ​ ( k − 1 ) ​ 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ X k m , n ​ ( X 0 m , n ) − δ ] ≤ 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ ( X − k m − k , n − k ) 1 − δ ] ≤ e t 0 ​ ( k + 1 ) ​ 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ X k m , n ​ ( X 0 m , n ) − δ ] . \begin{split}e^{t_{0}(k-1)}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[X_{k}^{m,n}(X_{0}^{m,n})^{-\delta}]&\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[(X_{-k}^{m-k,n-k})^{1-\delta}]\\
&\leq e^{t_{0}(k+1)}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[X_{k}^{m,n}(X_{0}^{m,n})^{-\delta}].\end{split} 
 
 (5.93) 
 
 Suppose first that  0 < ε < 1 0<\varepsilon<1  and
 0 ≤ δ ≤ ε 0\leq\delta\leq\varepsilon . Holder with conjugate exponents
 1 / ( 1 − ε ) 1/(1-\varepsilon)  and  1 / ε 1/\varepsilon  gives 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ ( X k m , n ) 1 − ε ] ≤ 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ X k m , n ​ ( X 0 m , n ) − δ ] 1 − ε × 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ ( X 0 m , n ) ( 1 − ε ) ​ δ / ε ] ε . \begin{split}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[(X_{k}^{m,n})^{1-\varepsilon}]&\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[X_{k}^{m,n}(X_{0}^{m,n})^{-\delta}]^{1-\varepsilon}\\
&\quad\times\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[(X_{0}^{m,n})^{(1-\varepsilon)\delta/\varepsilon}]^{\varepsilon}.\end{split} 
 
 (5.94) 
 
 Because  ( 1 − ε ) ​ δ / ε ≤ 1 − ε (1-\varepsilon)\delta/\varepsilon\leq 1-\varepsilon ,
Lyapunov’s inequality gives the explicit bound 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ ( X 0 m , n ) ( 1 − ε ) ​ δ / ε ] ε ≤ 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ ( X 0 m , n ) 1 − ε ] δ . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[(X_{0}^{m,n})^{(1-\varepsilon)\delta/\varepsilon}]^{\varepsilon}\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[(X_{0}^{m,n})^{1-\varepsilon}]^{\delta}. 
 
 (5.95) 
 
 Solving ( 5.93 ) for
 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ X k m , n ​ ( X 0 m , n ) − δ ] \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[X_{k}^{m,n}(X_{0}^{m,n})^{-\delta}] ,
raising the result to  1 − ε 1-\varepsilon , and substituting it and
( 5.95 ) into ( 5.94 ) proves ( 5.106 ) with the displayed factor
 e − t 0 ​ ( 1 − ε ) ​ k e^{-t_{0}(1-\varepsilon)k} , with the bounded factor
 e t 0 ​ ( 1 − ε ) e^{t_{0}(1-\varepsilon)}  absorbed into  C p C_{p} . When
 ε = 0 \varepsilon=0 , necessarily  δ = 0 \delta=0 , and
( 5.106 ) is the lower inequality in
( 5.93 ) solved for the right-hand expectation. When
 ε = 1 \varepsilon=1 , the left side is a probability and the assertion
used in the iteration follows from  𝟏 { X > 0 } ≤ 1 \mathbf{1}\{X>0\}\leq 1 . Thus no
division by the endpoint value  ε = 0 \varepsilon=0  or  1 1  is used. 
 
 
 We next prove the fractional up/down inequalities. The proof must use
increments of the allowed slab: the number of first entrances into an
extreme layer need not equal the complete connected count in that layer,
because an open path may move laterally after its first entrance. The
argument below is the site-coordinate version of
 [ 15 , Lemma 6.7]  and never makes that
identification. It suffices to consider the nontrivial parameter range 
 

 
 
 m ≤ min ⁡ { 0 , k } ≤ max ⁡ { 0 , k } ≤ n ; m\leq\min\{0,k\}\leq\max\{0,k\}\leq n; 
 
 
 outside this range the slab omits the root or target layer and the
left-hand side is zero. 
 
 
 First suppose that  m , n m,n  are finite. Fix a root  v v , an offset
 R v = x R_{v}=x , and an integer  ℓ ∈ [ k ∨ 0 , n ] \ell\in[k\vee 0,n] . Define the upward
increment 
 

 
 
 Z ℓ + := X k m , ℓ ​ ( v ) − X k m , ℓ − 1 ​ ( v ) , Z_{\ell}^{+}:=X_{k}^{m,\ell}(v)-X_{k}^{m,\ell-1}(v), 
 
 
 where the second term is defined to be zero when
 ℓ = k ∨ 0 \ell=k\vee 0 , if the smaller slab omits either the root layer or the
target layer. For  ℓ > 0 \ell>0 , let  A ℓ A_{\ell}  be the open cluster of
 v v  in  L m , ℓ − 1 ​ ( v ) L_{m,\ell-1}(v) ; for  ℓ = 0 \ell=0 , put
 A 0 = ∅ A_{0}=\varnothing . Reveal this cluster and then reveal the states of
its neighbors in  L ℓ ​ ( v ) L_{\ell}(v) . Let 
 

 
 
 W ℓ = { { v } , ℓ = 0 , { w ∈ L ℓ ( v ) : U w ≤ p , w ∼ A ℓ } , ℓ > 0 , N ℓ = | W ℓ | . W_{\ell}=\begin{cases}\{v\},&\ell=0,\\
\{w\in L_{\ell}(v):U_{w}\leq p,\ w\sim A_{\ell}\},&\ell>0,\end{cases}\qquad N_{\ell}=|W_{\ell}|. 
 
 
 Every member of  W ℓ W_{\ell}  is connected to  v v  in
 L m , ℓ ​ ( v ) L_{m,\ell}(v) . By the parameter restriction
 0 < p ≤ p c s 0<p\leq p_{c}^{\rm s}  fixed before ( 5.92 ), the full
 p p -cluster of  v v  is finite almost surely, and hence 
 

 
 
 N ℓ ≤ X ℓ m , ℓ ​ ( v ) < ∞ . N_{\ell}\leq X_{\ell}^{m,\ell}(v)<\infty. 
 
 (5.96) 
 
 
 
 Enumerate  W ℓ W_{\ell}  deterministically as  w 1 , … , w N ℓ w_{1},\ldots,w_{N_{\ell}} .
At step  i i , delete  A ℓ A_{\ell} , the other entry sites
 W ℓ ∖ { w i } W_{\ell}\setminus\{w_{i}\} , and all clusters assigned at earlier steps,
and expose the cluster  K i K_{i}  of  w i w_{i}  in the remaining part of
 L m , ℓ ​ ( v ) L_{m,\ell}(v) . (No earlier  K j K_{j}  contains  w i w_{i} , since every
other entry site was removed at step  j j .) Let
 Y i = | K i ∩ L k ​ ( v ) | Y_{i}=|K_{i}\cap L_{k}(v)| . Removing the other entry sites is essential:
it ensures that  w i w_{i}  is the only
coordinate conditioned open in this residual exploration. All edges
within  L ℓ ​ ( v ) L_{\ell}(v)  whose endpoints have not been removed are retained,
so vertices reached laterally in the extreme layer are included in the
appropriate  K i K_{i} . 
 
 
 The assigned sets cover the increment counted by  Z ℓ + Z_{\ell}^{+} . Indeed,
let  u ∈ L k ​ ( v ) u\in L_{k}(v)  be connected to  v v  in  L m , ℓ ​ ( v ) L_{m,\ell}(v) , but not
in  L m , ℓ − 1 ​ ( v ) L_{m,\ell-1}(v) , and choose a simple open path from  v v  to
 u u . If  ℓ = 0 \ell=0 , its initial vertex is  w 1 = v w_{1}=v . If
 ℓ > 0 \ell>0 , take the successor  w w  of the last vertex of the path in
 A ℓ A_{\ell} . This successor exists. It cannot belong to
 L m , ℓ − 1 ​ ( v ) L_{m,\ell-1}(v) , since an open neighbor there would also belong to
 A ℓ A_{\ell} ; hence  w ∈ W ℓ w\in W_{\ell} . On the remaining part of the path,
take the last vertex belonging to  W ℓ W_{\ell} , and denote it by
 w j w_{j} . The suffix from  w j w_{j}  to  u u  avoids both  A ℓ A_{\ell}  and
 W ℓ ∖ { w j } W_{\ell}\setminus\{w_{j}\} : if it re-entered  A ℓ A_{\ell} , it would have
to leave again before reaching  u u , and that later exit would pass
through a later member of  W ℓ W_{\ell} . If this suffix meets a cluster assigned
before step  j j , let  i < j i<j  be the least index of an assigned cluster
that it meets. The part of the suffix from that intersection to  u u 
avoids every  K r K_{r}  with  r < i r<i  and every other entry site, and hence
was available during the exploration of  K i K_{i} . Thus  u ∈ K i u\in K_{i} .
If the suffix meets no earlier cluster, it is available when  K j K_{j}  is
explored and  u ∈ K j u\in K_{j} . The sequential exploration therefore assigns
 u u  in every case. Consequently 
 

 
 
 Z ℓ + ≤ ∑ i = 1 N ℓ Y i . Z_{\ell}^{+}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\ell}}Y_{i}. 
 
 (5.97) 
 
 
 
 Let  ℱ ℓ \mathcal{F}_{\ell}  contain the exploration of  A ℓ A_{\ell} , the
revealed entry set  W ℓ W_{\ell} , and the fixed layer field, and let
 ℱ ℓ , i \mathcal{F}_{\ell,i}  also contain the first  i i  residual-cluster
explorations. Immediately before the exploration from  w i w_{i} , its root
is known open; every other available site is either untested
Bernoulli ( p ) (p)  or has been tested closed. The first-hit residual kernel
therefore couples this cluster below an independent open-root cluster in 
 

 
 
 L m − ℓ , 0 ​ ( w i ) = L m , ℓ ​ ( v ) , L_{m-\ell,0}(w_{i})=L_{m,\ell}(v), 
 
 
 where the equality uses the exact layer-shift rule. Consequently, on
the event  { i ≤ N ℓ } \{i\leq N_{\ell}\} , 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ Y i 1 − ε ∣ ℱ ℓ , i − 1 ] ≤ E ^ p m − ℓ , 0 ​ ( k − ℓ , 1 − ε ) . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[Y_{i}^{1-\varepsilon}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell,i-1}]\leq\widehat{E}_{p}^{m-\ell,0}(k-\ell;1-\varepsilon). 
 
 (5.98) 
 
 Here and below a conditional statement indexed by  i i  is asserted on
 { i ≤ N ℓ } \{i\leq N_{\ell}\} ; this event and  N ℓ N_{\ell}  are
 ℱ ℓ \mathcal{F}_{\ell} -measurable. Applying the tower property to
( 5.98 ) gives, on the same event, 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ Y i 1 − ε ∣ ℱ ℓ ] ≤ E ^ p m − ℓ , 0 ​ ( k − ℓ , 1 − ε ) . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[Y_{i}^{1-\varepsilon}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]\leq\widehat{E}_{p}^{m-\ell,0}(k-\ell;1-\varepsilon). 
 
 (5.99) 
 
 Put  a = ( 1 − ε ) ​ ( 1 − δ ) a=(1-\varepsilon)(1-\delta) . Subadditivity of
 t 1 − ε t^{1-\varepsilon} , conditional Jensen for the concave map
 t 1 − δ t^{1-\delta} , and ( 5.97 )–( 5.99 ) give 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( Z ℓ + ) a ∣ ℱ ℓ ] ≤ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( ∑ i = 1 N ℓ Y i 1 − ε ) 1 − δ | ℱ ℓ ] ≤ ( ∑ i = 1 N ℓ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ Y i 1 − ε ∣ ℱ ℓ ] ) 1 − δ ≤ N ℓ 1 − δ ​ [ E ^ p m − ℓ , 0 ​ ( k − ℓ , 1 − ε ) ] 1 − δ . \begin{split}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[(Z_{\ell}^{+})^{a}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]&\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}\!\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\ell}}Y_{i}^{1-\varepsilon}\right)^{1-\delta}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\right]\\
&\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\ell}}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[Y_{i}^{1-\varepsilon}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell}]\right)^{1-\delta}\\
&\leq N_{\ell}^{1-\delta}\left[\widehat{E}_{p}^{m-\ell,0}(k-\ell;1-\varepsilon)\right]^{1-\delta}.\end{split} 
 
 (5.100) 
 
 At an exponent zero, every power is interpreted using
 0 0 = 0 0^{0}=0  and  r 0 = 1 r^{0}=1  for  r > 0 r>0 ; the same inequalities follow
directly from the corresponding nonemptiness indicators. For the other
exponents, conditional Jensen with possibly infinite expectations is
obtained first for the bounded variable
 min ⁡ { M , ∑ i Y i 1 − ε } \min\{M,\sum_{i}Y_{i}^{1-\varepsilon}\}  and then by monotone
convergence as  M → ∞ M\to\infty . Thus no integrability is being assumed.
Taking
expectations in ( 5.100 ) and using ( 5.96 ) yields 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( Z ℓ + ) a ] ≤ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( X ℓ m , ℓ ​ ( v ) ) 1 − δ ] ​ [ E ^ p m − ℓ , 0 ​ ( k − ℓ , 1 − ε ) ] 1 − δ . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[(Z_{\ell}^{+})^{a}]\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[(X_{\ell}^{m,\ell}(v))^{1-\delta}]\left[\widehat{E}_{p}^{m-\ell,0}(k-\ell;1-\varepsilon)\right]^{1-\delta}. 
 
 (5.101) 
 
 
 
 The pointwise telescoping identity 
 

 
 
 X k m , n ​ ( v ) = ∑ ℓ = k ∨ 0 n Z ℓ + X_{k}^{m,n}(v)=\sum_{\ell=k\vee 0}^{n}Z_{\ell}^{+} 
 
 
 and subadditivity of  t a t^{a}  now give the first inequality below after
taking the supremum over  v , x v,x . 
 
 
 For the downward inequality, set 
 

 
 
 Z ℓ − := X k ℓ , n ​ ( v ) − X k ℓ + 1 , n ​ ( v ) , ℓ ∈ [ m , k ∧ 0 ] , Z_{\ell}^{-}:=X_{k}^{\ell,n}(v)-X_{k}^{\ell+1,n}(v),\qquad\ell\in[m,k\wedge 0], 
 
 
 with the second term zero at  ℓ = k ∧ 0 \ell=k\wedge 0  when its slab omits the
root or target layer. For  ℓ < 0 \ell<0 , let  A ℓ − A_{\ell}^{-}  be the open
cluster of  v v  in  L ℓ + 1 , n ​ ( v ) L_{\ell+1,n}(v) , reveal its neighbors in
 L ℓ ​ ( v ) L_{\ell}(v) , and put 
 

 
 
 W ℓ − = { w ∈ L ℓ ( v ) : U w ≤ p , w ∼ A ℓ − } . W_{\ell}^{-}=\{w\in L_{\ell}(v):U_{w}\leq p,\ w\sim A_{\ell}^{-}\}. 
 
 
 For  ℓ = 0 \ell=0 , put  A 0 − = ∅ A_{0}^{-}=\varnothing  and  W 0 − = { v } W_{0}^{-}=\{v\} .
Writing  N ℓ − = | W ℓ − | N_{\ell}^{-}=|W_{\ell}^{-}| , every entry is connected to  v v  in
 L ℓ , n ​ ( v ) L_{\ell,n}(v) , and therefore 
 

 
 
 N ℓ − ≤ X ℓ ℓ , n ​ ( v ) < ∞ . N_{\ell}^{-}\leq X_{\ell}^{\ell,n}(v)<\infty. 
 
 (5.102) 
 
 Enumerate the entries. For each entry  w i w_{i} , explore its cluster in
 L ℓ , n ​ ( v ) L_{\ell,n}(v)  after deleting  A ℓ − A_{\ell}^{-} , all other entries, and
the clusters assigned at earlier steps. Let
 Y i − = | K i − ∩ L k ​ ( v ) | Y_{i}^{-}=|K_{i}^{-}\cap L_{k}(v)| . Let  ℱ ℓ , 0 − \mathcal{F}_{\ell,0}^{-}  contain
the exploration of  A ℓ − A_{\ell}^{-} , the revealed entry set, and the layer
field, and let  ℱ ℓ , i − \mathcal{F}_{\ell,i}^{-}  additionally contain the first
 i i  residual explorations. 
 
 
 To verify coverage, take a simple open path witnessing a vertex counted
by  Z ℓ − Z_{\ell}^{-} . If  ℓ = 0 \ell=0 , start from  v v . If  ℓ < 0 \ell<0 , take
the successor of its last vertex in  A ℓ − A_{\ell}^{-} ; it belongs to
 W ℓ − W_{\ell}^{-} , since any open successor in  L ℓ + 1 , n ​ ( v ) L_{\ell+1,n}(v)  would
also belong to  A ℓ − A_{\ell}^{-} . Now take the last entry on the remaining
path, say  w j w_{j} . Its suffix avoids the upper cluster and every entry
other than  w j w_{j} : a later return to the upper cluster would require a
subsequent exit through another member of  W ℓ − W_{\ell}^{-} . If this suffix
meets a cluster assigned before step
 j j , let  i < j i<j  be the least index of such a cluster. The suffix from
an intersection with  K i − K_{i}^{-}  to the endpoint avoids all  K r − K_{r}^{-}  with
 r < i r<i , as well as the upper cluster and every deleted entry, so it was
present when  K i − K_{i}^{-}  was explored; the endpoint therefore lies in
 K i − K_{i}^{-} . If there is no such intersection, the whole suffix is present
when  K j − K_{j}^{-}  is explored and the endpoint lies in  K j − K_{j}^{-} . Hence 
 

 
 
 Z ℓ − ≤ ∑ i = 1 N ℓ − Y i − . Z_{\ell}^{-}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\ell}^{-}}Y_{i}^{-}. 
 
 (5.103) 
 
 The residual slab rooted at an entry  w i ∈ L ℓ ​ ( v ) w_{i}\in L_{\ell}(v)  is 
 

 
 
 L 0 , n − ℓ ​ ( w i ) = L ℓ , n ​ ( v ) , L_{0,n-\ell}(w_{i})=L_{\ell,n}(v), 
 
 
 and the first-hit residual domination gives, on
 { i ≤ N ℓ − } \{i\leq N_{\ell}^{-}\} , 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( Y i − ) 1 − ε ∣ ℱ ℓ , i − 1 − ] ≤ E ^ p 0 , n − ℓ ​ ( k − ℓ , 1 − ε ) . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[(Y_{i}^{-})^{1-\varepsilon}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\ell,i-1}^{-}]\leq\widehat{E}_{p}^{0,n-\ell}(k-\ell;1-\varepsilon). 
 
 
 The event  { i ≤ N ℓ − } \{i\leq N_{\ell}^{-}\}  and the value of  N ℓ − N_{\ell}^{-}  are
 ℱ ℓ , 0 − \mathcal{F}_{\ell,0}^{-} -measurable. The tower property therefore gives
the same bound conditional on  ℱ ℓ , 0 − \mathcal{F}_{\ell,0}^{-} , on that event.
Applying the three-line subadditivity and
conditional-Jensen calculation in ( 5.100 ), now with
 N ℓ − N_{\ell}^{-}  and  Y i − Y_{i}^{-} , and then using
( 5.102 )–( 5.103 ), gives the explicit increment
estimate 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( Z ℓ − ) a ] ≤ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( X ℓ ℓ , n ​ ( v ) ) 1 − δ ] ​ [ E ^ p 0 , n − ℓ ​ ( k − ℓ , 1 − ε ) ] 1 − δ . \widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[(Z_{\ell}^{-})^{a}]\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[(X_{\ell}^{\ell,n}(v))^{1-\delta}]\left[\widehat{E}_{p}^{0,n-\ell}(k-\ell;1-\varepsilon)\right]^{1-\delta}. 
 
 (5.104) 
 
 Finally, the pointwise identity 
 

 
 
 X k m , n ​ ( v ) = ∑ ℓ = m k ∧ 0 Z ℓ − , X_{k}^{m,n}(v)=\sum_{\ell=m}^{k\wedge 0}Z_{\ell}^{-}, 
 
 
 followed by subadditivity of  t a t^{a} , proves the second inequality after
taking the supremum over  v , x v,x . 
 
 
 If  m = − ∞ m=-\infty  or  n = ∞ n=\infty , fix  v , x v,x  and apply the finite-slab
inequalities with  m r = m ∨ ( − r ) m_{r}=m\vee(-r)  and  n r = n ∧ r n_{r}=n\wedge r . The count on
the left increases pointwise to its infinite-slab value. Each term on the
finite right-hand side is at most the corresponding term with the stated
infinite endpoint, by domain monotonicity, and the missing terms may be
padded with zeros. Monotone convergence on the left and Tonelli’s theorem
for the nonnegative series on the right therefore give the infinite-slab
inequality at this fixed  v , x v,x . Taking the supremum only afterwards
gives the displayed  E ^ p \widehat{E}_{p}  bounds. We have proved,
for  0 ≤ ε , δ ≤ 1 0\leq\varepsilon,\delta\leq 1  and
 − ∞ ≤ m ≤ k ≤ n ≤ ∞ -\infty\leq m\leq k\leq n\leq\infty , 
 

 
 
 
 E ^ p m , n ​ ( k , ( 1 − ε ) ​ ( 1 − δ ) ) \displaystyle\widehat{E}_{p}^{m,n}(k;(1-\varepsilon)(1-\delta)) 
 
 
 
 
 ≤ ∑ ℓ = k ∨ 0 n E ^ p m , ℓ ​ ( ℓ , 1 − δ ) ​ [ E ^ p m − ℓ , 0 ​ ( k − ℓ , 1 − ε ) ] 1 − δ , \displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{\ell=k\vee 0}^{n}\widehat{E}_{p}^{m,\ell}(\ell;1-\delta)\left[\widehat{E}_{p}^{m-\ell,0}(k-\ell;1-\varepsilon)\right]^{1-\delta}, 
 
 
 
 
 E ^ p m , n ​ ( k , ( 1 − ε ) ​ ( 1 − δ ) ) \displaystyle\widehat{E}_{p}^{m,n}(k;(1-\varepsilon)(1-\delta)) 
 
 
 
 
 ≤ ∑ ℓ = m k ∧ 0 E ^ p ℓ , n ​ ( ℓ , 1 − δ ) ​ [ E ^ p 0 , n − ℓ ​ ( k − ℓ , 1 − ε ) ] 1 − δ . \displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{\ell=m}^{k\wedge 0}\widehat{E}_{p}^{\ell,n}(\ell;1-\delta)\left[\widehat{E}_{p}^{0,n-\ell}(k-\ell;1-\varepsilon)\right]^{1-\delta}. 
 
 (5.105) 
 
 These are the two fractional up/down inequalities, with all parameter
ranges displayed and with no endpoint factor. In particular, the proof
uses only the inequalities in ( 5.96 ), not an equality between
the number of entry sites and the complete extreme-layer count. 
 
 
 For completeness, Holder applied after ( 5.30 ) gives the other nonlinear
input. For  k ∈ ℤ k\in\mathbb{Z} ,  0 ≤ δ ≤ ε < 1 0\leq\delta\leq\varepsilon<1 , and
 − ∞ ≤ m ≤ min ⁡ { 0 , k } ≤ max ⁡ { 0 , k } ≤ n ≤ ∞ -\infty\leq m\leq\min\{0,k\}\leq\max\{0,k\}\leq n\leq\infty , 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ ( X k m , n ) 1 − ε ] ≤ C p ​ e − t 0 ​ ( 1 − ε ) ​ k ​ 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ ( X − k m − k , n − k ) 1 − δ ] 1 − ε ​ 𝔼 ^ p ​ [ ( X 0 m , n ) 1 − ε ] δ . \begin{split}&\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[(X_{k}^{m,n})^{1-\varepsilon}]\\
&\quad\leq C_{p}e^{-t_{0}(1-\varepsilon)k}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[(X_{-k}^{m-k,n-k})^{1-\delta}]^{1-\varepsilon}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}[(X_{0}^{m,n})^{1-\varepsilon}]^{\delta}.\end{split} 
 
 (5.106) 
 
 At  ε = 1 \varepsilon=1  the assertion used in the iteration is trivial.
Formula ( 5.92 ) shows that the negative power in the MTP proof is evaluated
only on a positive count. We make no pointwise mass-transport assertion
after conditioning on  R v = x R_{v}=x ; fixed-offset estimates are obtained from
the averaged identity only through ( 5.107 )–( 5.108 ) below.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Proposition 5.4   (Critical fractional closure) . 
 
 Assume the critical peak-survival estimate ( 5.91 ) and the
fractional layer recursions of
Proposition  5.3 . At
 p = p c s p=p_{c}^{\rm s} , the starting-slab estimate ( 5.112 ), the
excess-width estimates ( 5.118 ), the half-space estimates
( 5.123 ), and the full-space estimate ( 5.34 ) hold
for every  0 < ε ≤ 1 0<\varepsilon\leq 1  and every  ζ > 0 \zeta>0 , uniformly in
the root and fixed layer offset. 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The logical order within the proof is the following. The deterministic
fixed-offset and shift comparisons ( 5.107 )–( 5.109 )
are proved first. Peak survival ( 5.91 ), together with the
earlier layer estimates, then yields the starting-slab estimate
( 5.112 ). Only after that estimate is available do we derive
the excess-width bounds ( 5.118 ). The fractional recursions are
then applied using these established bounds to prove the half-space and
full-space conclusions. Thus ( 5.112 ) and
( 5.118 ) are conclusions of this proposition, not hypotheses. 
 
 
 Two deterministic comparisons complete the list of inputs to the
fractional iteration. If  0 ≤ a ≤ 1 0\leq a\leq 1 , then 
 

 
 
 E ^ p m , n ​ ( k , a ) ≤ sup v ∑ j = − 1 1 𝔼 ^ p v ​ [ ( X k + j m − 1 , n + 1 ​ ( v ) ) a ] , \widehat{E}_{p}^{m,n}(k;a)\leq\sup_{v}\sum_{j=-1}^{1}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v}[(X_{k+j}^{m-1,n+1}(v))^{a}], 
 
 (5.107) 
 
 where the expectations on the right average the independent layer offset.
Indeed, couple two offsets  x , y ∈ [ 0 , 1 ) x,y\in[0,1)  using the same site
configuration. Directly from the half-open definition ( 5.6 ), 
 

 
 
 L k x ​ ( v ) ⊆ L k − 1 , k + 1 y ​ ( v ) , L m , n x ​ ( v ) ⊆ L m − 1 , n + 1 y ​ ( v ) . L_{k}^{x}(v)\subseteq L_{k-1,k+1}^{y}(v),\qquad L_{m,n}^{x}(v)\subseteq L_{m-1,n+1}^{y}(v). 
 
 
 Thus, pointwise in the site configuration and in  y y , the fixed-offset
count is at most the sum of the three counts on the right of ( 5.107 ).
Use  ( r + s + t ) a ≤ r a + s a + t a (r+s+t)^{a}\leq r^{a}+s^{a}+t^{a} , integrate  y y , and then take the
supremum over  v , x v,x . This proves ( 5.107 ), including its one-layer slab
enlargement. 
 
 
 Combining ( 5.107 ) with the offset-averaged inequality ( 5.106 ) gives
the following explicit fixed-offset consequence. If
 0 ≤ δ ≤ ε < 1 0\leq\delta\leq\varepsilon<1  and
 − ∞ ≤ m ≤ min ⁡ { 0 , k } ≤ max ⁡ { 0 , k } ≤ n ≤ ∞ -\infty\leq m\leq\min\{0,k\}\leq\max\{0,k\}\leq n\leq\infty , then 
 

 
 
 E ^ p m , n ​ ( k , 1 − ε ) ≤ C p ​ ∑ j = − 1 1 e − t 0 ​ ( 1 − ε ) ​ ( k + j ) × [ E ^ p m − k − j − 1 , n − k − j + 1 ​ ( − k − j , 1 − δ ) ] 1 − ε × [ E ^ p m − 1 , n + 1 ​ ( 0 , 1 − ε ) ] δ . \begin{split}\widehat{E}_{p}^{m,n}(k;1-\varepsilon)&\leq C_{p}\sum_{j=-1}^{1}e^{-t_{0}(1-\varepsilon)(k+j)}\\
&\quad\times\left[\widehat{E}_{p}^{m-k-j-1,n-k-j+1}(-k-j;1-\delta)\right]^{1-\varepsilon}\\
&\quad\times\left[\widehat{E}_{p}^{m-1,n+1}(0;1-\varepsilon)\right]^{\delta}.\end{split} 
 
 (5.108) 
 
 To verify ( 5.108 ), apply ( 5.106 ) separately to the  j j -th averaged
term in ( 5.107 ), with parameters
 ( m − 1 , n + 1 , k + j ) (m-1,n+1,k+j) , and bound each resulting averaged moment by the
fixed-offset supremum ( 5.31 ). This is the only passage from the
offset-averaged mass transport to fixed-offset quantities. 
 
 
 Also, if
 0 < p ≤ 1 0<p\leq 1 ,
 m ≤ 0 ≤ n m\leq 0\leq n ,  m + r ≤ 0 ≤ n + r m+r\leq 0\leq n+r , and
 m ≤ k ≤ n m\leq k\leq n , forcing a connector across  | r | |r|  adjacent layers
gives 
 

 
 
 E ^ p m + r , n + r ​ ( k + r , a ) ≤ e C p ​ | r | ​ E ^ p m , n ​ ( k , a ) . \widehat{E}_{p}^{m+r,n+r}(k+r;a)\leq e^{C_{p}|r|}\widehat{E}_{p}^{m,n}(k;a). 
 
 (5.109) 
 
 Here is the finite-energy argument for ( 5.109 ). Choose
 w ∈ L r ​ ( v ) w\in L_{r}(v)  by iterating the maximal-increment oriented edge fixed in
( 5.7 ), or its reverse when  r < 0 r<0 . The resulting monotone path
 γ \gamma  from  v v  to  w w  has exactly  | r | |r|  non-root sites.
The two assumptions that both shifted slabs contain layer zero ensure
that  γ \gamma  lies in  L m + r , n + r ​ ( v ) L_{m+r,n+r}(v) . Equivariance of the offset
field gives 
 

 
 
 L j ​ ( w ) = L j + r ​ ( v ) for every  ​ j , L_{j}(w)=L_{j+r}(v)\quad\text{for every }j, 
 
 
 exactly, by the half-open convention in ( 5.6 ). On
 { γ ​  open } \{\gamma\text{ open}\} , every
site counted by  X k + r m + r , n + r ​ ( v ) X_{k+r}^{m+r,n+r}(v)  is therefore counted by
 X k m , n ​ ( w ) X_{k}^{m,n}(w) . We spell out the conditioning that yields the connector
factor. Put 
 

 
 
 Y = ( X k + r m + r , n + r ​ ( v ) ) a , Z = ( X k m , n ​ ( w ) ) a , Y=(X_{k+r}^{m+r,n+r}(v))^{a},\qquad Z=(X_{k}^{m,n}(w))^{a}, 
 
 
 and let  G γ G_{\gamma}  be the event that the  | r | |r|  non-root sites of
 γ \gamma  are open. Under  ℙ ^ p v , x \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x} ,
 ℙ ^ p v , x ​ ( G γ ) = p | r | \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p}^{v,x}(G_{\gamma})=p^{|r|} . Both  Y Y  and
 𝟏 G γ \mathbf{1}_{G_{\gamma}}  are increasing functions of the remaining
Bernoulli coordinates, so conditional site FKG and the pointwise
inequality  Y ≤ Z Y\leq Z  on  G γ G_{\gamma}  give 
 

 
 
 p | r | ​ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ Y ≤ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ Y ; G γ ] ≤ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ Z ; G γ ] . p^{|r|}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}Y\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[Y;G_{\gamma}]\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[Z;G_{\gamma}]. 
 
 (5.110) 
 
 To remove the conditioning at the other endpoint, let
 G γ all G_{\gamma}^{\rm all}  denote the event that every site of  γ \gamma ,
including  v v , is open, and let  𝔼 p x \mathbb{E}_{p}^{x}  denote expectation
for the unconditioned Bernoulli labels with the layer offset at  v v 
fixed to  x x . Since  G γ all G_{\gamma}^{\rm all}  implies that  w w  is open, 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ Z ; G γ ] = p − 1 ​ 𝔼 p x ​ [ Z ; G γ all ] ≤ p − 1 ​ 𝔼 p x ​ [ Z ; w ​  open ] = 𝔼 ^ p w , R w ​ Z . \begin{split}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[Z;G_{\gamma}]&=p^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{p}^{x}[Z;G_{\gamma}^{\rm all}]\\
&\leq p^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{p}^{x}[Z;w\text{ open}]=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{w,R_{w}}Z.\end{split} 
 
 (5.111) 
 
 The offset is independent of all Bernoulli labels and therefore
contributes no factor in either equality. Combining
( 5.110 ) and ( 5.111 ) gives 
 

 
 
 p | r | ​ 𝔼 ^ p v , x ​ [ ( X k + r m + r , n + r ​ ( v ) ) a ] ≤ 𝔼 ^ p w , R w ​ [ ( X k m , n ​ ( w ) ) a ] . p^{|r|}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{v,x}[(X_{k+r}^{m+r,n+r}(v))^{a}]\leq\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p}^{w,R_{w}}[(X_{k}^{m,n}(w))^{a}]. 
 
 
 Taking the root/offset supremum and writing
 C p = | log ⁡ p | C_{p}=|\log p|  proves ( 5.109 ). No coordinate that was conditioned
closed is reopened, and these comparisons use no unexplored conditional
law. 
 
 
 We now give the iteration rather than cite its intermediate names. First
we prove the quantified starting-slab estimate: for every
 0 < ε ≤ 1 0<\varepsilon\leq 1  and  ζ > 0 \zeta>0 , 
 

 
 
 E ^ p c s − k − r , 0 ​ ( − k , 1 − ε ) ≤ C ε , ζ ​ exp ⁡ [ ζ ​ k + C 0 ​ r ] ( k , r ≥ 0 ) . \widehat{E}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{-k-r,0}(-k;1-\varepsilon)\leq C_{\varepsilon,\zeta}\exp[\zeta k+C_{0}r]\quad(k,r\geq 0). 
 
 (5.112) 
 
 To derive it, we first display the peak-to-layer transport, keeping the
offset averaged as required by ( 5.30 ). Put  K = k + ℓ K=k+\ell ,
 S = K + r + 3 S=K+r+3 , and, for  i = 0 , 1 , 2 , 3 i=0,1,2,3 , define 
 

 
 
 F i ( a , b ) = 𝟏 { b ∈ L − K − i ( a ) , a ↔ b  in  L − K − r − 3 , 0 ( a ) , 𝒫 a ( S ) } . F_{i}(a,b)=\mathbf{1}\{b\in L_{-K-i}(a),\ a\leftrightarrow b\text{ in }L_{-K-r-3,0}(a),\ \mathcal{P}_{a}(S)\}. 
 
 
 This transport is diagonally invariant and vanishes unless both
endpoints are open. Hence, for every fixed root  v v , ( 5.30 ) gives 
 

 
 
 p c s ​ ∫ 0 1 𝔼 ^ p c s v , x ​ [ X − K − i − K − r − 3 , 0 ​ ( v ) ; 𝒫 v ​ ( S ) ] ​ d x = 𝔼 p c s ​ ∑ a F i ​ ( a , v ) ​ Δ ​ ( v , a ) . \begin{split}p_{c}^{\rm s}\int_{0}^{1}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{v,x}[X_{-K-i}^{-K-r-3,0}(v);\mathcal{P}_{v}(S)]\,dx=\mathbb{E}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}\sum_{a}F_{i}(a,v)\Delta(v,a).\end{split} 
 
 (5.113) 
 
 If  F i ​ ( a , v ) = 1 F_{i}(a,v)=1 , the exact layer shift gives 
 

 
 
 a ∈ L K + i ​ ( v ) , a ↔ v ​  in  ​ L i − r − 3 , K + i ​ ( v ) , L − S , ∞ ​ ( a ) = L i − r − 3 , ∞ ​ ( v ) . a\in L_{K+i}(v),\qquad a\leftrightarrow v\text{ in }L_{i-r-3,K+i}(v),\qquad L_{-S,\infty}(a)=L_{i-r-3,\infty}(v). 
 
 
 There is at most one such  a a : two candidates are connected in the
last half-space, whereas each peak condition says that its candidate is
the unique vertex of maximal modular height in that component. Also
 a ∈ L K + i ​ ( v ) a\in L_{K+i}(v)  implies
 Δ ⁡ ( v , a ) ≤ e t 0 ​ ( K + i + 1 ) \Delta(v,a)\leq e^{t_{0}(K+i+1)} . Dropping the peak event and applying
( 5.42 ), with lower depth  r + 3 − i r+3-i , now yields 
 

 
 
 𝔼 p c s ​ ∑ a F i ​ ( a , v ) ​ Δ ​ ( v , a ) ≤ C ​ e − t 0 ​ ( α p c s s − 1 ) ​ K + C 0 ​ r . \mathbb{E}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}\sum_{a}F_{i}(a,v)\Delta(v,a)\leq Ce^{-t_{0}(\alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}-1)K+C_{0}r}. 
 
 
 Thus ( 5.113 ), summed over  i = 0 , 1 , 2 , 3 i=0,1,2,3 , proves the genuinely
offset-averaged estimate 
 

 
 
 ∫ 0 1 ∑ i = 0 3 𝔼 ^ p c s v , x ​ [ X − k − ℓ − i − k − ℓ − r − 3 , 0 ; 𝒫 v ​ ( k + ℓ + r + 3 ) ] ​ 𝑑 x ≤ C ​ e − t 0 ​ ( α p c s s − 1 ) ​ ( k + ℓ ) + C 0 ​ r . \int_{0}^{1}\sum_{i=0}^{3}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{v,x}[X_{-k-\ell-i}^{-k-\ell-r-3,0};\mathcal{P}_{v}(k+\ell+r+3)]\,dx\leq Ce^{-t_{0}(\alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}-1)(k+\ell)+C_{0}r}. 
 
 
 In particular, for each  v v  there is an offset  x v x_{v}  for which the
integrand is no larger than the right side. Since
 ℙ ^ v , x ​ ( 𝒫 v ​ ( S ) ) ≥ ( 1 − p c s ) d \widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{v,x}(\mathcal{P}_{v}(S))\geq(1-p_{c}^{\rm s})^{d}  for every  x x , division converts this semicolon
estimate at  x v x_{v}  into the conditional numerator in ( 5.76 ). The
same offset has the uniform denominator lower bound ( 5.91 ). We may
therefore take  ( v , x v ) (v,x_{v})  in the infimum in ( 5.76 ). This is the only
fixed-offset selection in the argument; no tilted MTP is asserted after
fixing an offset. Combining these facts gives, for every auxiliary
 ξ > 0 \xi>0 , 
 

 
 
 sup v , x 𝔼 ^ p c s v , x ​ [ X − k − k − r , 0 ; 𝒬 v ; k , ℓ , r ] ≤ C ξ ​ e C 0 ​ r − t 0 ​ ( α p c s s − 1 ) ​ k + t 0 ​ ξ ​ ℓ . \sup_{v,x}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{v,x}[X_{-k}^{-k-r,0};\mathcal{Q}_{v;k,\ell,r}]\leq C_{\xi}e^{C_{0}r-t_{0}(\alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}-1)k+t_{0}\xi\ell}. 
 
 (5.114) 
 
 Indeed, ( 5.91 ) bounds the reciprocal denominator by
 C ξ ​ e t 0 ​ ( α p c s s − 1 + ξ ) ​ ℓ C_{\xi}e^{t_{0}(\alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}-1+\xi)\ell} .
This proves ( 5.114 ), including the sign and every parameter. Since
( 5.73 ) gives  α p c s s ≥ 1 \alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}\geq 1 , its negative
 k k -term may be discarded. On the complementary event, the root crosses
from the bottom of the slab to layer  ℓ − 1 \ell-1 . Applying ( 5.42 ) with
target  ℓ − 1 \ell-1  and lower depth  k + r + 2 k+r+2 , and then using
 α p c s s ≥ 1 \alpha_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{\rm s}\geq 1 , gives explicitly 
 

 
 
 sup v , x ℙ ^ p c s v , x ​ ( 𝒬 v ; k , ℓ , r c ) ≤ C ​ e C 0 ​ ( k + r ) − t 0 ​ ℓ . \sup_{v,x}\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{v,x}(\mathcal{Q}_{v;k,\ell,r}^{c})\leq Ce^{C_{0}(k+r)-t_{0}\ell}. 
 
 (5.115) 
 
 
 
 For fixed  v , x v,x  and an integer  N ≥ 2 N\geq 2 , split 
 

 
 
 ℙ ^ p c s v , x ​ ( X − k − k − r , 0 ≥ N ) ≤ N − 1 ​ 𝔼 ^ p c s v , x ​ [ X − k − k − r , 0 ; 𝒬 ] + ℙ ^ p c s v , x ​ ( 𝒬 c ) . \widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{v,x}(X_{-k}^{-k-r,0}\geq N)\leq N^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{v,x}[X_{-k}^{-k-r,0};\mathcal{Q}]+\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{v,x}(\mathcal{Q}^{c}). 
 
 (5.116) 
 
 Choose  C 1 > C 0 / t 0 C_{1}>C_{0}/t_{0}  and set
 ℓ = max ⁡ { 2 , ⌈ t 0 − 1 ​ log ⁡ N + C 1 ​ ( k + r ) ⌉ } \ell=\max\{2,\lceil t_{0}^{-1}\log N+C_{1}(k+r)\rceil\} .
Then ( 5.115 ) makes the second term at most  C ​ N − 1 CN^{-1} , since 
 

 
 
 C 0 ​ ( k + r ) − t 0 ​ ℓ ≤ − log ⁡ N − ( t 0 ​ C 1 − C 0 ) ​ ( k + r ) + O ⁡ ( 1 ) . C_{0}(k+r)-t_{0}\ell\leq-\log N-(t_{0}C_{1}-C_{0})(k+r)+O(1). 
 
 
 After discarding the nonpositive  k k -term in ( 5.114 ), the first term
in ( 5.116 ) is at most 
 

 
 
 C ξ ​ N − 1 + ξ ​ exp ⁡ [ t 0 ​ ξ ​ C 1 ​ k + ( C 0 + t 0 ​ ξ ​ C 1 ) ​ r ] . C_{\xi}N^{-1+\xi}\exp[t_{0}\xi C_{1}k+(C_{0}+t_{0}\xi C_{1})r]. 
 
 
 Given  θ , ζ > 0 \theta,\zeta>0 , choose  ξ > 0 \xi>0  so that
 ξ ≤ θ \xi\leq\theta  and  t 0 ​ ξ ​ C 1 ≤ ζ t_{0}\xi C_{1}\leq\zeta , and enlarge the fixed
coefficient  C 0 C_{0}  of  r r  once. The last two displays imply

 (5.117) 
 
 Take  θ = ε / 2 \theta=\varepsilon/2 . For an integer-valued  X ≥ 0 X\geq 0 , 
 

 
 
 𝔼 ​ X 1 − ε ≤ 1 + C ε ​ ∑ N ≥ 1 N − ε ​ ℙ ​ ( X ≥ N ) . \mathbb{E}X^{1-\varepsilon}\leq 1+C_{\varepsilon}\sum_{N\geq 1}N^{-\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(X\geq N). 
 
 
 Multiplying the first term of ( 5.117 ) by  N − ε N^{-\varepsilon} 
produces  N − 1 − ε / 2 N^{-1-\varepsilon/2} , and the second produces
 N − 1 − ε N^{-1-\varepsilon} ; both series converge. The factors independent of
 N N  are exactly  e ζ ​ k + C 0 ​ r e^{\zeta k+C_{0}r} , after one final enlargement of
the constant. This proves ( 5.112 ), with all parameters chosen before
 v , x , k , r v,x,k,r . 
 
 
 We next prove the stronger, excess-uniform version of the upward
estimate retained in
 [ 15 , proof of Lemma 6.10] . With the same
order of quantifiers, for every  0 < ε ≤ 1 0<\varepsilon\leq 1  and
 ζ > 0 \zeta>0 , there is a constant  C ⋆ < ∞ C_{\star}<\infty , independent of
 ε , ζ , k , r \varepsilon,\zeta,k,r , such that 
 
 
 
 
 E ^ p c s − r , k + r ​ ( k , 1 − ε ) \displaystyle\widehat{E}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{-r,k+r}(k;1-\varepsilon) 
 ≤ C ε , ζ ​ e − ( t 0 − ζ ) ​ k + C ⋆ ​ r , \displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon,\zeta}e^{-(t_{0}-\zeta)k+C_{\star}r}, 
 
 (5.118) 
 
 
 E ^ p c s − ∞ , r ​ ( 0 , 1 − ε ) \displaystyle\widehat{E}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{-\infty,r}(0;1-\varepsilon) 
 ≤ C ε ​ e C ⋆ ​ r . \displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{C_{\star}r}. 
 
 
 Let  C sh C_{\rm sh}  be the coefficient of  | r | |r|  in the exponential
shift loss ( 5.109 ), evaluated at  p = p c s p=p_{c}^{\rm s} .
For the first inequality, choose  0 < ε 0 < ε 0<\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon , then
choose  0 < δ ≤ ε 0 0<\delta\leq\varepsilon_{0}  and  0 < ξ ≤ 1 0<\xi\leq 1  so that 
 

 
 
 t 0 ​ ε 0 + C sh ​ δ + 2 ​ ξ < ζ . t_{0}\varepsilon_{0}+C_{\rm sh}\delta+2\xi<\zeta. 
 
 (5.119) 
 
 Apply the fixed-offset consequence ( 5.108 ) with
 ( m , n ) = ( − r , k + r ) (m,n)=(-r,k+r) . For  η ∈ ( 0 , 1 ] \eta\in(0,1] , put 
 

 
 
 Q η = E ^ p c s − k − 2 ​ r − 2 , 0 ​ ( − k − r − 1 , 1 − η ) . Q_{\eta}=\widehat{E}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{-k-2r-2,0}(-k-r-1;1-\eta). 
 
 
 For  j = − 1 , 0 , 1 j=-1,0,1 , the first fractional factor in
( 5.108 ) is 
 

 
 
 E ^ p c s − r − k − j − 1 , r − j + 1 ​ ( − k − j , 1 − δ ) . \widehat{E}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{-r-k-j-1,r-j+1}(-k-j;1-\delta). 
 
 
 It is the shift by  r − j + 1 r-j+1  of  Q δ Q_{\delta} . The last factor is
 E ^ p c s − r − 1 , k + r + 1 ​ ( 0 , 1 − ε 0 ) \widehat{E}_{p_{c}^{\rm s}}^{-r-1,k+r+1}(0;1-\varepsilon_{0}) , which is the shift by  k + r + 1 k+r+1  of
