 (7.117) 
 
 Put  s = ( log ⁡ m ) 2 / 3 s=(\log m)^{2/3} . 
 
 
 Because  ℒ ⁡ ( G , 20 ) \mathscr{L}(G,20)  was defined on all real outer scales, this
is a genuine dichotomy even though  s s  need not be an integer. If
 s ∉ ℒ ⁡ ( G , 20 ) s\notin\mathscr{L}(G,20) , its defining universal condition fails, so
there is  t ∈ [ s 1 / 3 , s ] t\in[s^{1/3},s]  for which the strict reverse inequality
below holds. Take the integer test radius  b = ⌈ s ⌉ b=\lceil s\rceil . For all

 1 ≤ b ≤ m 1 / 3 / 8 1\leq b\leq m^{1/3}/8 , and monotonicity gives

&\leq(\log m)^{-1}\end{split} 
 
 
 for large  n 0 n_{0} , since  b = ( log ⁡ m ) 2 / 3 + O ⁡ ( 1 ) = o ⁡ ( log ⁡ m ) b=(\log m)^{2/3}+O(1)=o(\log m) . 
 
 
 Suppose instead that  s ∈ ℒ ⁡ ( G , 20 ) s\in\mathscr{L}(G,20) . For large  n 0 n_{0} ,

 (7.118) 
 
 Define  Gr − 1 ⁡ ( x ) = min ⁡ { r : Gr G ⁡ ( r ) ≥ x } \operatorname{Gr}^{-1}(x)=\min\{r:\operatorname{Gr}_{G}(r)\geq x\}  and take

 (7.119) 
 
 Submultiplicativity of ball volume and the bounded one-step volume ratio
give 
 

 
 
 log ⁡ Gr G ⁡ ( b ) ≥ c ​ ( log ⁡ log ⁡ m ) 9 , log ⁡ Gr G ⁡ ( 10 ​ b + 1 ) ≤ 3 ​ ( log ⁡ m ) 1 / 10 + C d . \log\operatorname{Gr}_{G}(b)\geq c(\log\log m)^{9},\qquad\log\operatorname{Gr}_{G}(10b+1)\leq 3(\log m)^{1/10}+C_{d}. 
 
 (7.120) 
 
 For the lower bound, if the first term realizes the minimum, use
 Gr ⁡ ( 5 ​ b ) ≥ 5 ​ b + 1 \operatorname{Gr}(5b)\geq 5b+1 ; if the inverse-growth term realizes
it, use its defining inequality. In either case
 log ⁡ Gr ⁡ ( 5 ​ b ) ≥ ( log ⁡ log ⁡ m ) 9 \log\operatorname{Gr}(5b)\geq(\log\log m)^{9}  for large  m m , and
then use

 Gr ⁡ ( 3 ​ R ) ≤ Gr ⁡ ( R ) 3 ≤ d 3 ​ e 3 ​ ( log ⁡ m ) 1 / 10 \operatorname{Gr}(3R)\leq\operatorname{Gr}(R)^{3}\leq d^{3}e^{3(\log m)^{1/10}} . 
 
 
 Since  s s  is a low-growth scale,
 log ⁡ Gr ⁡ ( s ) ≤ ( log ⁡ s ) 20 < ( log ⁡ m ) 1 / 10 \log\operatorname{Gr}(s)\leq(\log s)^{20}<(\log m)^{1/10} . Thus ( 7.119 ) also gives, for large  m m , 
 

 
 
 5 ​ b ≥ s , 8 ​ b ≤ e ( log ⁡ s ) 10 , 10 ​ b ≤ 1 2 ​ m 1 / 3 . 5b\geq s,\qquad 8b\leq e^{(\log s)^{10}},\qquad 10b\leq\tfrac{1}{2}m^{1/3}. 
 
 (7.121) 
 
 For  a , c ∈ S 4 ​ b a,c\in S_{4b} , a path through the root has length at most  8 ​ b 8b 
and lies in  B 4 ​ b B_{4b} . Its  s s -neighborhood lies in

( 7.121 ) therefore imply

 (7.122) 
 
 Apply Auxiliary Lemma  6.A  with radii
 4 ​ b , 10 ​ b , N = m 1 / 3 4b,10b,N=m^{1/3} , then Lemma  6.1  with
exponent  1 / 4 1/4 . Equations ( 7.120 )–( 7.122 ) yield

 Also ( 7.120 ) and ( 7.115 ) give

 This proves ( 7.117 ) in both cases. 
 
 
 By  𝖥 i \mathsf{F}_{i}  and transitivity,
 κ p i s ​ ( m , ∞ ) ≥ e − T i \kappa_{p_{i}}^{\rm s}(m,\infty)\geq e^{-T_{i}} . Moreover
 T i ≤ 3 ​ log ⁡ log ⁡ m T_{i}\leq 3\sqrt{\log\log m}  by ( 7.115 ).
Proposition  7.3 , ( 7.117 ), and
 λ ≥ 10 / c out \lambda\geq 10/c_{\rm out}  now give either

or the bound  e − 3 ​ T i = e − T i + 1 e^{-3T_{i}}=e^{-T_{i+1}}  required by
 𝖢 i + 1 \mathsf{C}_{i+1} . This proves the second line of ( 7.116 ). 
 
 
 The Full-Space implication.  The numbers  n j n_{j}  are real, but
membership in  ℒ ⁡ ( G , 20 ) \mathscr{L}(G,20)  is defined for real outer scales. If
 n j ∈ ℒ ⁡ ( G , 20 ) n_{j}\in\mathscr{L}(G,20) , then  𝖢 j + 1 \mathsf{C}_{j+1}  applies at
 m = n j m=n_{j} , and
 e ( log ⁡ n j ) 10 ≥ 2 ​ n j + 1 e^{(\log n_{j})^{10}}\geq 2n_{j+1} , so it directly gives
 𝖥 j + 1 \mathsf{F}_{j+1} . If  n j ∉ ℒ ⁡ ( G , 20 ) n_{j}\notin\mathscr{L}(G,20) , failure of its
defining universal condition supplies a real scale
 r ∈ [ n j 1 / 3 , n j ] r\in[n_{j}^{1/3},n_{j}]  with
 log ⁡ Gr ⁡ ( r ) > ( log ⁡ r ) 20 \log\operatorname{Gr}(r)>(\log r)^{20} . Apply Proposition 6.2
along a geodesic of length at most  2 ​ n j + 1 2n_{j+1} , using balls whose
radius is the explicit integer  ρ = ⌊ r / 3 ⌋ \rho=\lfloor r/3\rfloor , the endpoint lower
bound  e − T j e^{-T_{j}} ,
the sub-sprinkle 
 

 
 
 p j ′ = Spr ⁡ ( p j ; T j − 1 ) ≤ p j + 1 , p_{j}^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spr}(p_{j};T_{j}^{-1})\leq p_{j+1}, 
 
 
 and ghost intensity  h = h j h=h_{j}  from ( 7.113 ). Submultiplicativity supplies
 | B ρ | ≥ h − 1 |B_{\rho}|\geq h^{-1} . Moreover, the union of two balls at consecutive
geodesic vertices has diameter at most  2 ​ ρ + 1 ≤ n j 2\rho+1\leq n_{j} , so
 𝖥 j \mathsf{F}_{j}  gives the adjacent-set hypothesis of Proposition 6.2.
The chain contains at most  2 ​ n j + 1 + 1 2n_{j+1}+1  sets. If
 p j + 1 < p c s p_{j+1}<p_{c}^{\rm s} , then throughout the relevant sprinkling
interval there is no infinite cluster, and
 ℙ q ​ ( Piv s ⁡ [ 1 , R ] ) ↓ 0 \mathbb{P}_{q}(\operatorname{Piv}^{\rm s}[1,R])\downarrow 0  as
 R → ∞ R\to\infty , uniformly in  q q : the pivotal event is bounded by the
event that one of the at most  d d  neighboring clusters reaches distance
 R − 1 R-1 , which is dominated by its probability at the subcritical upper
endpoint. Hence one may choose a finite thickening radius  R R  with
 h ​ R ≥ 1 hR\geq 1  that satisfies the pivotal hypothesis of Proposition 6.2.
Since the base domain here is all of  V V , its value does not enlarge the
output domain. If instead
 p j + 1 ≥ p c s p_{j+1}\geq p_{c}^{\rm s} , the alternative in ( 7.116 ) already holds.
The baseline part of  δ 0 \delta_{0}  is  T 0 − 1 T_{0}^{-1} , so this sub-sprinkle
is available also when  j = 0 j=0 . Equation ( 7.113 ) verifies the chain-length
and error hypotheses. The conclusion is at least
 c ′ ​ e − 2 ​ T j ≥ e − 3 ​ T j = e − T j + 1 c^{\prime}e^{-2T_{j}}\geq e^{-3T_{j}}=e^{-T_{j+1}} , first at  p j ′ p_{j}^{\prime}  and then
at  p j + 1 p_{j+1}  by monotonicity. For  j = 0 j=0 ,
 𝖥 0 \mathsf{F}_{0}  is stronger than the endpoint bound  e − T 0 e^{-T_{0}}  used
here. This proves the third line. In each line the alternative is
introduced before snowballing, so all uses of Proposition 6.2 occur
below  p c s p_{c}^{\rm s} . 
 
 
 We now complete the proof of
Proposition  7.1 . Apply those implications
with  n 0 = n n_{0}=n  and  p 0 = p p_{0}=p , using the sequences just defined in ( 7.114 ).
Either some
 p i ≥ p c s p_{i}\geq p_{c}^{\rm s} , or the
Full-Space lower bound propagates at every  n i n_{i} . In the latter case,
if  p ∞ < p c s p_{\infty}<p_{c}^{\rm s} , site sharpness
 [ 2 , Theorems 2–3 and Section 6]  would give
 ℙ p ∞ ​ ( u ↔ v ) ≤ e − c ​ d ​ ( u , v ) \mathbb{P}_{p_{\infty}}(u\leftrightarrow v)\leq e^{-cd(u,v)} ,
contradicting the propagated lower bound
 e − ( log ⁡ log ⁡ n i ) 1 / 2 e^{-(\log\log n_{i})^{1/2}} . Thus  p c s ≤ p ∞ p_{c}^{\rm s}\leq p_{\infty} .
The sprinkling semigroup and the geometric series for  δ i \delta_{i}  give 
 

 
 
 p ∞ ≤ Spr ( p ; 2 ( log log n ) − 1 / 2 + K Burn s ( G , n , p ) ) , p_{\infty}\leq\operatorname{Spr}\left(p;2(\log\log n)^{-1/2}+K\operatorname{Burn}^{\rm s}(G,n,p)\right), 
 
 
 which is ( 7.64 ).  □ \square 
 
 
 
 8  Proof of the main theorem 

 
 Proof of Theorem  1.1 . 
 
 Let  G n → G G_{n}\to G , and pass to a tail on which the degrees equal  d d  and
 p c s ​ ( G n ) ≤ 1 − b p_{c}^{\rm s}(G_{n})\leq 1-b . 
 
 
 1.  Lemma  2.4  gives
 p c s ​ ( G ) ≤ lim inf n p c s ​ ( G n ) p_{c}^{\rm s}(G)\leq\liminf_{n}p_{c}^{\rm s}(G_{n}) . 
 
 
 2.  If  G G  has polynomial growth, use the site case of the
Contreras–Martineau–Tassion locality theorem
 [ 7 , Theorem 1.1] . 
 
 
 3.  If the canonical action
 Aut ⁡ ( G ) ↷ G \operatorname{Aut}(G)\curvearrowright G  is nonunimodular, use
Theorem  5.1  for  G G  and the eventual
nonunimodular tail
 G n G_{n} , always with the canonical actions
 Aut ⁡ ( G ) \operatorname{Aut}(G)  and  Aut ⁡ ( G n ) \operatorname{Aut}(G_{n}) , in the
height-locality argument ( 5.130 )–( 5.131 ). 
 
 
 4.  Otherwise the canonical action of  G G  is
unimodular and  G G  has superpolynomial growth. This branch includes
every canonically unimodular graph, even if it admits some other closed
transitive nonunimodular subgroup. The canonical actions of  G n G_{n}  are
eventually unimodular. The approximating graphs are also eventually
not one-dimensional. Indeed, if an
infinite locally finite graph satisfies  | B R ​ ( o ) | ≤ C ​ R |B_{R}(o)|\leq CR  for all
 R ≥ 1 R\geq 1 , then
 | B R ​ ( o ) | = 1 + ∑ j = 1 R | S j ​ ( o ) | |B_{R}(o)|=1+\sum_{j=1}^{R}|S_{j}(o)|  shows that infinitely many of its
pairwise disjoint spheres have cardinality at most
 M := ⌈ 2 ​ C ⌉ M:=\lceil 2C\rceil ; otherwise the displayed sum eventually exceeds
 C ​ R CR . Select such spheres  ( Σ i ) i ≥ 1 (\Sigma_{i})_{i\geq 1} . Every
 Σ i \Sigma_{i}  separates  o o  from infinity. For every  p < 1 p<1 , let
 E i E_{i}  be the event that every site of  Σ i \Sigma_{i}  is closed. Their
vertex-disjointness makes the events
 E i E_{i}  independent, while
 ℙ p ​ ( E i ) = ( 1 − p ) | Σ i | ≥ ( 1 − p ) M > 0 \mathbb{P}_{p}(E_{i})=(1-p)^{|\Sigma_{i}|}\geq(1-p)^{M}>0 .
Therefore  ∑ i ℙ p ​ ( E i ) = ∞ \sum_{i}\mathbb{P}_{p}(E_{i})=\infty , and the second
Borel–Cantelli lemma says that infinitely many  E i E_{i} ’s occur almost
surely. Every infinite path from the root must meet every separating
cutset, so the occurrence of even one  E i E_{i}  blocks an infinite open
root path. Hence  p c s = 1 p_{c}^{\rm s}=1  for every transitive graph of
linear growth. This contradicts
 p c s ​ ( G n ) ≤ 1 − b p_{c}^{\rm s}(G_{n})\leq 1-b . If upper semicontinuity failed, pass to a
subsequence and set 
 

 
 
 p ∗ := inf n p c s ​ ( G n ) > p c s ​ ( G ) , p 0 = 1 2 ​ ( p ∗ + p c s ​ ( G ) ) . p_{*}:=\inf_{n}p_{c}^{\rm s}(G_{n})>p_{c}^{\rm s}(G),\qquad p_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl(p_{*}+p_{c}^{\rm s}(G)\bigr). 
 
 
 Let  m ⁡ ( R ) m(R)  be such that  B R ​ ( G j ) ≅ B R ​ ( G ) B_{R}(G_{j})\cong B_{R}(G)  for
 j ≥ m ⁡ ( R ) j\geq m(R) . We claim that 
 

 
 
 lim R → ∞ sup q ∈ [ p 0 , p ∗ ] sup j ≥ m ⁡ ( R ) Burn s ⁡ ( G j , R , q ) = 0 . \lim_{R\to\infty}\sup_{q\in[p_{0},p_{*}]}\sup_{j\geq m(R)}\operatorname{Burn}^{\rm s}(G_{j},R,q)=0. 
 
 (8.1) 
 
 To prove this, fix  m ∈ ℒ ⁡ ( G j , 20 ) ∩ [ log ⁡ R , R ] m\in\mathscr{L}(G_{j},20)\cap[\sqrt{\log R},R]  and put  N m = ⌊ m 1 / 3 ⌋ N_{m}=\lfloor m^{1/3}\rfloor . The
low-growth definition and monotonicity give, for large  m m ,
 log ⁡ | B N m ​ ( G j ) | ≤ ( log ⁡ N m ) 21 \log|B_{N_{m}}(G_{j})|\leq(\log N_{m})^{21} ; the harmless extra power
accounts for the floor in  N m N_{m} . Apply the low-growth
version ( 6.10 ) with  K = 21 K=21 ,  ε = 1 / 8 \varepsilon=1/8 , and outer radius
 N m N_{m} , uniformly for  q ∈ [ p 0 , p ∗ ] q\in[p_{0},p_{*}] . If  c 0 = c 0 ​ ( d , p 0 ) > 0 c_{0}=c_{0}(d,p_{0})>0 
is its inner-radius constant, choose  c < c 0 / 16 c<c_{0}/16 . Since
 4 ​ ⌊ c ​ log ⁡ m ⌋ ≤ c 0 ​ log ⁡ N m 4\lfloor c\log m\rfloor\leq c_{0}\log N_{m}  for all sufficiently
large  m m , 
 

 
 
 ℙ q ​ ( Piv s ⁡ [ 4 ​ ⌊ c ​ log ⁡ m ⌋ , N m ] ) ≤ C ​ ( ( log ⁡ N m ) 21 N m ) 3 / 8 ≤ ( log ⁡ m ) − 1 . \mathbb{P}_{q}(\operatorname{Piv}^{\rm s}[4\lfloor c\log m\rfloor,N_{m}])\leq C\left(\frac{(\log N_{m})^{21}}{N_{m}}\right)^{3/8}\leq(\log m)^{-1}. 
 
 
 Consequently 
 

 
 
 b s ​ ( m , q ) ≥ c ​ log ⁡ m b_{\rm s}(m,q)\geq c\log m 
 
 (8.2) 
 
 after decreasing  c c  to meet the upper restriction in its
definition. Since  c ​ log ⁡ m ≤ R c\log m\leq R , local agreement gives 
 

 
 
 | B ⌊ c ​ log ⁡ m ⌋ ​ ( G j ) | = | B ⌊ c ​ log ⁡ m ⌋ ​ ( G ) | . |B_{\lfloor c\log m\rfloor}(G_{j})|=|B_{\lfloor c\log m\rfloor}(G)|. 
 
 
 Trofimov’s near-polynomial growth theorem
 [ 25 , Theorem 1]  says that a connected
vertex-transitive graph whose ball volumes are bounded by one fixed
polynomial along an unbounded sequence of radii has polynomial growth.
Its contrapositive, applied to the non-polynomial-growth graph  G G ,
implies 
 

 
 
 inf t ≥ c ​ log ⁡ log ⁡ R log ⁡ | B t ​ ( G ) | log ⁡ t ⟶ ∞ . \inf_{t\geq c\log\sqrt{\log R}}\frac{\log|B_{t}(G)|}{\log t}\longrightarrow\infty. 
 
 (8.3) 
 
 Equations ( 8.2 )–( 8.3 ), monotonicity of ball volume, and
 log ⁡ m / log ⁡ log ​ m → ∞ \log m/\log\log m\to\infty , uniformly for
 m ≥ log ⁡ R m\geq\sqrt{\log R} , show that every term in the supremum ( 7.1 )
tends to zero uniformly in  q , j q,j . This proves ( 8.1 ). 
 
 
 By its definition,  p 0 > p c s ​ ( G ) p_{0}>p_{c}^{\rm s}(G) . Separately, the elementary
simple-path bound gives
 p c s ​ ( G ) ≥ 1 / ( d − 1 ) > 1 / d p_{c}^{\rm s}(G)\geq 1/(d-1)>1/d . Apply
Proposition  7.1  with  a = 1 / d a=1/d  and
with the tail gap  b b , and let  K , N K,N  be its constants. For  R ≥ N R\geq N  and
 j ≥ m ⁡ ( R ) j\geq m(R) , set 
 

 
 
 ε j , R = 2 ( log log R ) − 1 / 2 + K Burn s ( G j , R , p 0 ) . \varepsilon_{j,R}=2(\log\log R)^{-1/2}+K\operatorname{Burn}^{\rm s}(G_{j},R,p_{0}). 
 
 
 By ( 8.1 ),  sup j ≥ m ⁡ ( R ) ε j , R → 0 \sup_{j\geq m(R)}\varepsilon_{j,R}\to 0 . Hence there
is  R 0 ≥ N R_{0}\geq N  such that, for every  R ≥ R 0 R\geq R_{0}  and
 j ≥ m ⁡ ( R ) j\geq m(R) , 
 

 
 
 ε j , R ≤ 1 , Spr ⁡ ( p 0 ; ε j , R ) < p ∗ . \varepsilon_{j,R}\leq 1,\qquad\operatorname{Spr}(p_{0};\varepsilon_{j,R})<p_{*}. 
 
 
 The first inequality also bounds the smaller  δ 0 \delta_{0}  appearing
in Proposition  7.1 , and the second
implies its required upper bound
 1 − b 1-b , since  p ∗ ≤ p c s ​ ( G j ) ≤ 1 − b p_{*}\leq p_{c}^{\rm s}(G_{j})\leq 1-b . 
 
 
 Fix  c tail > 0 c_{\rm tail}>0  so small that
 2 ​ c tail ​ log ⁡ ( 2 / p 0 ) < 1 / 4 2c_{\rm tail}\log(2/p_{0})<1/4 , and for each  k ≥ 1 k\geq 1  set 
 

 
 
 R k = ⌈ c tail ​ log ⁡ k ⌉ . R_{k}=\lceil c_{\rm tail}\log k\rceil. 
 
 
 For every sufficiently large  k k ,  R k ≥ R 0 R_{k}\geq R_{0} . Since
 p c s ​ ( G j ) ≥ p ∗ p_{c}^{\rm s}(G_{j})\geq p_{*} , the conclusion of
Proposition  7.1 
at scale  R k R_{k}  is false for every  j ≥ m ⁡ ( R k ) j\geq m(R_{k}) . Its
contrapositive therefore gives
vertices  u , v ∈ B R k u,v\in B_{R_{k}} 
such that 
 

 
 
 ℙ p 0 G j ​ ( u ↔ v ) < η ⁡ ( R k ) := exp ⁡ [ − log ⁡ log ⁡ ( ( log ⁡ R k ) 1 / 2 ) ] . \mathbb{P}_{p_{0}}^{G_{j}}(u\leftrightarrow v)<\eta(R_{k}):=\exp\!\left[-\sqrt{\log\log((\log R_{k})^{1/2})}\right]. 
 
 
 Since  p 0 < p ∗ ≤ p c s ​ ( G j ) p_{0}<p_{*}\leq p_{c}^{\rm s}(G_{j}) , site percolation at  p 0 p_{0} 
on  G j G_{j}  has no infinite cluster. Hence
Lemma  4.1 , applied to  u , v u,v  and a
geodesic of length at most  2 ​ R k 2R_{k} , yields 
 

 
 
 ℙ p 0 G j ( | K o | ≥ k ) 2 ≤ C d , p 0 R k ( 2 / p 0 ) 2 ​ R k + 1 k − 1 / 2 + η ( R k ) . \mathbb{P}_{p_{0}}^{G_{j}}(|K_{o}|\geq k)^{2}\leq C_{d,p_{0}}R_{k}(2/p_{0})^{2R_{k}+1}k^{-1/2}+\eta(R_{k}). 
 
 (8.4) 
 
 The choice of  c tail c_{\rm tail}  makes the first term
 O ( k − 1 / 4 ) O(k^{-1/4}) , after enlarging the implicit constant to absorb the
logarithmic factor, while  η ⁡ ( R k ) → 0 \eta(R_{k})\to 0 . Hence the
left side tends to zero uniformly over  j ≥ m ⁡ ( R k ) j\geq m(R_{k}) . But
 p 0 > p c s ​ ( G ) p_{0}>p_{c}^{\rm s}(G) , so
 θ p 0 s ​ ( G ) > 0 \theta_{p_{0}}^{\rm s}(G)>0 . Choose  k k  so large that the bound
in ( 8.4 ) is smaller than
 θ p 0 s ​ ( G ) 2 / 4 \theta_{p_{0}}^{\rm s}(G)^{2}/4 , and then choose
 j ≥ m ⁡ ( R k ) j\geq m(R_{k})  so large that  B k ​ ( G j ) ≅ B k ​ ( G ) B_{k}(G_{j})\cong B_{k}(G) . The event
 { | K o | ≥ k } \{|K_{o}|\geq k\}  is local: a breadth-first exploration discovers
 k k  cluster vertices or stops after inspecting only
 B k − 1 ​ ( o ) B_{k-1}(o) . Hence the rooted  k k -ball isomorphism gives
 ℙ p 0 G j ​ ( | K o | ≥ k ) = ℙ p 0 G ​ ( | K o | ≥ k ) \mathbb{P}_{p_{0}}^{G_{j}}(|K_{o}|\geq k)=\mathbb{P}_{p_{0}}^{G}(|K_{o}|\geq k) . An open path from  o o  to
 B k ​ ( o ) c B_{k}(o)^{c}  contains at least  k + 1 k+1  distinct vertices. Repeating
the local-event equality in the comparison itself gives 
 

 
 
 ℙ p 0 G j ​ ( | K o | ≥ k ) \displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{p_{0}}^{G_{j}}(|K_{o}|\geq k) 
 = ℙ p 0 G ​ ( | K o | ≥ k ) \displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{p_{0}}^{G}(|K_{o}|\geq k) 
 
 
 
 
 ≥ ℙ p 0 G ​ ( o ↔ B k c ) ≥ θ p 0 s ​ ( G ) , \displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{p_{0}}^{G}(o\leftrightarrow B_{k}^{c})\geq\theta_{p_{0}}^{\rm s}(G), 
 
 
 contradicting ( 8.4 ). 
 
 
 Thus 
 

 
 
 lim sup n p c s ​ ( G n ) ≤ p c s ​ ( G ) . \limsup_{n}p_{c}^{\rm s}(G_{n})\leq p_{c}^{\rm s}(G). 
 
 
 Together with Step 1, this completes the proof.
∎

      support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
      methods listed below: 
     
       Click the "Report Issue"  ( 
             
           )  button, located in the page header. 
     
     Tip:  You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report. 
     Our team has already identified  the following issues . We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
      may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
      should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
      all. 
     Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a  list of packages that need conversion , and welcome  developer contributions .
